THE BELGRAVIAN

OCTOBER 1985

the official gazette of belgrave harriers

Front Cover. Injured for much of the season John Regis has managed to peak for the big ones and has produced an incredible list of performances including; Winning the Junior AAA's indoor 200m in a CBP, winning the U.K. Senior 200m, gaining an individual bronze in the European Junior 100m, a team gold in the short relay at the same Championship and culminating with a new Club record for the 200m of 20.78. Photo. Bill Laws.

John RegisBill Laws

Above. Mike Small has strengthened our throws squad this summer and in the hammer has moved into second place in our all-time rankings with 49.82. Add to this highly respectable performances in the shot, discus and javelin and it is clear that he will be an essential part of our 'A' teams bid for Division 1 next year.

Mike Small

Editorial · page 3

'MAMMOTH' FAREWELL

This mammoth edition of the Belgravian represents for your Editor a final fling. For after 6 years and 12 editions it is time for a new face.

As we progress towards our Centenary year we are moving into a period when naturally there will be a great deal of reminiscing about past glories. But in the role as Editor, where every aspect of Club activities is scrutinised and sifted before being recorded, it has become apparent that the Belgrave of today is in many ways far removed from that of 30 or even 10 years ago.

I well remember 6 years ago searching desparately for decent copy on athletic achievements. Apart from the up and coming young squad of Dave North, Mark Sinclair, Mark Waller, Paul Richley, Stuart Paton and John Gladwin, plus the ever successful walkers , there was very little to give hope for the future. Our track team was about to be relegated to Div. 2 of the Southern League and the Road and Country team were desparately short of star performers. Today all that has been transformed. The problem with producing the Club magazine now is that in all Sections there has been a massive increase in active members and an incredible increase in athletic performances. Unfortunately because costs and Editorial time have to be kept within reasonable limits many items now have to be omitted.

Behind all this success has been a growing team of enthusiastic officials and helpers all willing to give generously of their time to help our young, active and up and coming members. It is because of them that we can look forward to a well planned and exciting future. A future that will see many of our members rising to the top levels of International competition with all the financial rewards which such achievements deserve. I hope that the Belgravian will continue to record these successes and will provide interesting and stimulating reading for all members whether young or old, rabbits or stars. I wish my successor well. The place of our magazine in Club life cannot be overestimated.

Dave NorthMark SinclairMark WallerPaul RichleyStuart PatonJohn Gladwin

Column · page 4

grapevine

Leo Coy hit the airwaves in January when he appeared as the final questioner on Radio 4's Sports Review and Quiz.

Graham Macky has written from New Zealand. He and Debbie were married last year and it suits him. After a fine second place in the N.Z. Marathon Championship he ran in the IAAF World Championship in Hiroshima on 14th April and finished 46th in 2:16.17. We also hear that there will be a new addition to the family early in 1986. Congratulations.

Dave Mote's tendon operation proved successful and he has now to decide whether to resume his athletes career or become a full time dilettante. The omens are not good. His 25th birthday Toga party at Belgrave Hall showed all the usual signs of creeping decadence. The North brothers also displayed a couple of dirty habits despite which their Fan Club organised by our younger Ladies seems to be growing!

John Aska at Walton track in September. Nothing strange you may think. But no' Our field events man has transformed into a top American Football star. Nicknamed 'Crusher' he helped a G.B. team defeat France 7-0 in the first International between these two countries. Doesn't qualify for the International Honours Board though!

Remember Paul Richley and Kevin Sturman left our shores for U.S. Universities in August '84. Almost immediately both suffered injuries which put them out of action for the season. Thereafter their lives followed strangely similar patterns. Kevin married Anita in May 1985 and its doubtful if we will see them back here on a permanent basis. Paul returned early in 1985 and announced his engagement to Tina Couzens. Congratulations and best wishes to all.

You'll read elsewhere of Mike Alleynes great 400m improvements this year. It may be a coincidence but at the middle of the year he got engaged. We wish him and Erica all happiness in the future.

But in 1984 the wedding was that of Chris Everett to founding member of our Ladies Section, Jane Pritchard. Most of us can't remember exactly what happened at the celebrations and its costing an arm and a leg to suppress Brian's video. Chris has moved to the Bahamas to further his career as a croupier. Jane joined him in the summer. Maggie Thatcher and The Queen followed in October!

February 14th - Valentine's Day saw a light aircraft circling South London/N.E.Surrey with an appropriate message in tow. The local press finally tracked down the instigator. Yes you've guessed - Charlie Manning! Can we afford for this boy to take charge of our Centenary Year promotional campaign?

Leo CoyGraham MackyDebbieDave MoteJohn AskaPaul RichleyKevin SturmanAnitaTina CouzensMike AlleynesEricaChris EverettJane PritchardBrianMaggie ThatcherCharlie Manning

Report · page 5

GORDON DOUBLEDAY

Gordon Doubleday, now happily ensconced (S.P's word of the month!) in Bath frequently writes to let us know what a great place it is. Gordon is very active timekeeping at local meetings. Here we see Gordon warming up for a big local event. He is caught in this shot red-handed with one of the local beauties - none other Diana Moran - more famous as TV's keep fit star the 'Green Goddess'. There had to be some perks!

Since moving to Bath -Gordon has been very much involved with Somer A.C. which was founded in 1981. His vast experience has been put to wide use in aid of local athletics and recently his services to Somer has been formally recognised by granting to him Life Membership. Gordon reports that several ex-Belgravians are active in the County of Avon and that the leading light behind Somer is non other than a man who was competing for us in the late 1960's - Bob James who sends his best wishes to those whose memories can stretch back that far.

Gordon DoubledayS.P.Diana MoranBob James

Report · page 5

A BUM DEAL!

Have you noticed that since the jogging boom dogs have become a lot more tolerant towards your average runner. There remain a few who find the nippier mortal irresistable and one of these had a good go at our top distance man Oliver Foote just at the start of the track season. The setting was the Kingston-By-Pass, Oliver was working up a nice pace mid-way between the Coombe Lane Bridge and the Robin Hood Roundabout when a particular ferocious specimen hurtled out of the riding stables and sank its nashers into his rear end. Our hero limped along to Kingston Hospital where the injury was much admired by the duty nurses as they drew lots to administer the compulsory anti-tetanus injection. Next on to the local cop shop where the matter was investigated by the boys in blue who eventually located the animal plus its owner. Negotiations for compensation began. No doubt Seb Coe would have commanded a good fee. our man settled for £28.

As it turned out the adventure had its brighter side. We now notice that Oliver has acquired a noticeable acceleration - a change of pace which led to a 58 sec. last lap as he gained the Surrey 5000m Silver medal in mid-May (and in a p.b.). This fact has not gone unnoticed by some of our middle distance coaches and it is rumoured that as dust falls on Wimbledon Common, some can be heard practising their barking and growling techniques - based on second hand instruction books by Percy Edwards. So next time you think you're being chased by a Doberman - check twice - it might be Jim Webster!!!!!

Oliver FooteSeb CoePercy EdwardsJim Webster

In the October 1985 Edition of Running magazine regular columnist Geoffrey Cannon actually referred to the Belgravian and mentioned how in the past he enjoyed reading of the successes of his son Matthew. Many readers will remember Matt. Cannon who established several Club age group records in the mid 1970's. His father Geoffrey joined Belgrave for a brief period. Geoffrey also writes for the Times and it is rewarding to receive his kind comments.

Obituary · page 6

LESLIE J. COHEN

W.E. Lucas

In Memorium

LESLIE J. COHEN

Long, lean and hungry looking and striding purposefully over a cross country course dressed in 'Wellies' and oilskins - that is how I prefer to remember him, for that was when he was at his happiest.

Born in South London in October 1908, he first graced the athletic scene in the '20s as a member of Ashcombe (later to be Ashcombe St. Georges AC), but it was as a Belgrave Harrier (from 1934) that he flourished into an excellent middle distance runner of near international standard with pre-war successes on track, road and country.

After the War he threw himself into athletic administration with such enthusiasm and energy that he was known to everyone in the sport for his organising ability. For many years he was secretary and/or championship secretary to the AAA, Southern AAA, Southern Cross Country Association, South of the Thames Cross Country Association, CAU and London Business Houses AA, besides other appointments associated with those organisations and with Belgrave Harriers, who are forever grateful for his fund raising initiative, which led to the development of their headquarters in Wimbledon to the standard they are today.

All this work led him, perhaps a little reluctantly, to the presidencies of Belgrave, South of the Thames, Southern AAA, Southern CCA, ECCU, and ultimately to a life vice presidency of the AAA.

It is a privilege to have been associated with this man and to have competed and served with him over a long period of time. We have missed him during the past few years when his health prevented him from carrying on in his usual energetic and spirited way, but he was not forgotten. Wherever his colleagues met his name cropped up and will continue to do so - we will remember him.

Our sympathies and condolences go to his wife Mary (nee Hutchins), who in her early days was also a distinguished athlete with Spartan Ladies.

W.E. Lucas,

Leslie J. CohenMary CohenW.E. Lucas

Obituary · page 6

ETHEL M. CARTER

On 10th October we lost one of our dearest and best loved supporters. Ethel who two years ago suffered a partial stroke and had been making a steady recovery, passed away peacefully. We have lost a great friend. Ethel was keenly interested in all of Belgrave's activities starting back in the 1930's when she first met Tom and encouraged him in his many successes. Tom and Ethel have been a part of the Belgrave scene for over 50 years providing a constant source of help and encouragement to generations of young athletes. Floral tributes both from the Club and from over 40 Belgravians said far more than words to convey our deep respect and love for Ethel. Our thoughts and support are with Tom at this sad time.

Ethel M. CarterTom

Obituary · page 6

BAS. COLLINS

Our Road Racing team members of the late 60's are now dispersed to many parts of the world. All will be saddened to learn of the death, while out on a training run, of Bas Collins. As a member of Surrey AC Bas. won the Surrey Senior CC title in 1964 and in 1967 in the AAA's Marathon Championship he finished 4th in 2:19.22. Bas joined H.W. in 1968 and was part of the teams which provided us with so many close relay races. After a lay off Bas re-started racing as a Vet. Many of his friends questioned his wisdom as it was rumoured he had a heart problem. But his love of the sport won. As David Cocksedge wrote in Athletes Weekly - at the age of 44 he died in the way he probably would have wanted.

Bas CollinsDavid Cocksedge

Feature · page 7

GORDON BISCOE - PRESIDENT

Gordon Biscoe joined Belgrave in 1953 after a period of service with the R.A.F. His athletics career began at school and continued during R.A.F. Service where he shared in many team successes over the country, including the winning of the Shropshire Championship in 1952. Although Gordon's career with Belgrave never hit the dizzy heights of individual stardom, he was one of the engine room men sharing in the successes of the 50's and 60's teams, when you had to fight to get into the Surrey, Southern,National and major relays.

Gordon's first love was always the road, achieving much better success than in the track where his best times were 2.06 for 880 yds, 4.35 for the Mile and just inside 15m for 3 Miles. In humerous vein he lists amongst his achievements on the road as being the only known person to run the famous 'Bridges Race' on the Thames Embankment in two left shoes; and being selected the most number of times as first reserve for the Brighton Relay!

His first official role in the Club was as Road Running Captain from 1964 to 1967, a period when our senior teams were the scourge of the road, when he then took over from Derek Crooks as Road Secretary.

In 1969 he then took over from Bill Laws as Cross Country Secretary, a position he has now held for 17 years. In addition during the late 60's he was Social Secretary for 2 years, when he organised a number of successful socials.

Gordon has always preferred to devote what time he has got to Belgrave rather than seek honours in the outside world of athletics, but he has served on the general committee of the Surrey AAA as well as the South of the Thames C.C.A. for many years, culminating this service by being elected President of the South of the Thames in 1983/84. He He is also a graded timekeeper.

His organisational abilities are now well known having built up a large experience (and store of amusing anecdotes) over the years, from organising ordinary club races to major championships on behalf of the Club. He organised our 20m Road Race for 6 years with the 1975 event being his most memorable one, as being the occasion he first met Margaret, now his wife, who at the time was Centre Organiser of the Wimbledon Red Cross who always attend this event. When they were married in 1979 Gordon confessed that it was then he realised he wasn't running fast enough any more! Like many Belgrave wives Margaret now helps out in many 'behind the scenes' activities, such as making cross country marker flags, or collating race entries etc. Gordon's longest running epic is now the Belgrave 12 Stage Road Relay which he has masterminded for 17 years in to the premier race that it is today carrying Southern Counties AAA Championship status and commercial sponsorship.

Away from Belgrave, Gordon is a Chartered Electrical Engineer working for a research association.

In his spare time, what there is left from Belgrave duties, business commitments (often abroad) and helping look after a young son, Richard of fourteen months, Gordon lists amongst his hobbies, gardening and Jazz - having been a very keen follower of Chris Barber and Humphrey Lyttleton.

Gordon BiscoeDerek CrooksBill LawsMargaretRichardChris BarberHumphrey Lyttleton

Editorial · page 8

From the President

GORDON BISCOE

May I first take this opportunity to say a sincere thank you to you all for the honour of being elected President of this great club; it is one which I shall always cherish.

In my seventeen years as Cross Country Secretary some previous Presidents have called for efforts to be made to achieve some athletic goal during their term of office, like winning the National Cross Country Championship, but while these goals are of course always desirable, they have sometimes been unrealistic. During this coming year I would, therefore, like to set a goal for all members, which is perhaps less spectacular in the short term, but is very desirable, very necessary and at the same time achievable - it is for us all to work harder and closer together to make Belgrave even greater. It is desirable in that we will be in our 99th year and what better way to celebrate the following centenary year and fulfil all the plans being made than with a thriving club.

It is necessary for, as most of you know, in the world of athletics times are changing fast and in order to keep pace with these changes it must be our objective to work for a stronger and more financially secure club with ever improving facilities to meet the needs of all our athletes.

It will mean forgetting some of the parochial attitudes that sometimes exist between the various sections, and working for the Club as a whole; it will mean recruiting 'new blood' to our organizational and management ranks to ease the burden of some of these already in office; and for our active athletes, particularly 'A Team' members, it will sometimes mean putting Club requirements before personal desires, in order to get team success. It is as teams that we will invariably get our victories and we are capable of far more than we do, if only our teams were properly supported. Success breeds success! and surely our efforts in the British League this summer are a fitting example.

Good Luck and best wishes in all your efforts in the year ahead.

GORDON BISCOE

Gordon Biscoe

Report · page 8

CRACKER!

There's a story doing the rounds that may well be apocryphal but is worth telling nonetheless. Following a substantial reduction in weight and victories in three road races in Italy, Marcello Bizio has become hot property. If one of the first team men is missing Marcello is your man. Unfortunately all this activity brought on a groin strain which has required surgery. Just before the operation Marcello was contacted by our Assistant Cross Country Sec. who urged him to turn out for the following weeks race. Now Marcello's English is improving by leaps and bounds. "Sorry", said our hero, "I can't run, I am with Hernia". "Don't worry old son", says Colin, "First claim membership of a foreign Club doesn't count!".

What a cracker! Anyway what we're trying to say is "Get well soon Marcello? The team needs you back as soon as possible"

Marcello BizioColin

Report · page 9

BELGRAVE HALL

Although Mike Winch and Judy Oakes had started operations at the new Gym as far back as October 1984 it wasn't until February that the rest of the Hall had been finished to a standard ready for the official opening.The Official Opening Ceremony was conducted by AAA's Secretary - Mike Farrell who was able to confirm that the AAA's would be contributing specialist equipment for strength training.Local dignitaries including the Major and Majoress of Wimbledon were in attendance and we were particularly pleased to welcome representatives of the Southern AAA's, the London and South East Regional Sports Council,the London and Surrey playing Fields Association. Present too was our biggest supporter - Peter Pitt-who you will recall stepped in at the last minute to provide a GLC grant of £14000 when the development project was foundering due to cost escallation and the introduction of VAT mid way through the the building programme.

Past Presidents travelled from all over the UK to see the conclusion of a project that many had helped start with the purchase of the Hall way back in the 1930's. All were able to admire the new developments which have resulted in the Hall now being used for some 70 hours per week and open for training 7 days each week.

Mike WinchJudy OakesMike FarrellPeter Pitt

Notice · page 9

OFFICIAL CHANGES

This years Annual General Meeting saw a substantial change round in our Official line-up. Joe Coleman fresh from an active Presidential year has taken over the mantle of Gen. Hon. Sec. while Eric Crutcher backed by Keith Duckett has taken over the role of Gen. Hon. Sec. Social. Harry Porter has extended his involvement with the Ladies Section taking over the Track and Field Sec. job as well as the Country while on the Vets side Keith Sinclair has moved into the Secretaries seat and we are delighted to see a new face on the management side. Elected members included on the Committee are; Dave Mote, Andy Paton,Keith Duckett,Sue Parrott and Bill Laws.

Bill Couzens. With Bill Couzens stepping down from the Gen. Sec. Admin. job we see an end of an era. It is difficult to overstate Bill's contribution to the improvement in Belgrave's fortunes over the last 5 years. It was he alone who built up the bar activities and the hall letting from nothing to a point where they provided a major contribution to the overall monies coming into the Club. In addition to running the business side Bill also undertook all the odd jobs needed to keep the facilities in good repair. It was not unusual for him to be called out of bed during the small hors of the morning and most Saturday mornings until quite recently he could be seen cleaning the Hall and generally undertaking those thousand and one jobs needed to keep everything running smoothly. Spare moments at home were taken up with book-keeping but probably Bill's best efforts were reserved the beer itself. Without doubt the best drop of Guiness in South London can be had at the Hall and that does not happen by accident.

It is a measure of Bill's contribution that his job has been split into four parts because it would be practically impossible to find anyone who could devote enough time to what in reality could be considered a full time job. The Club owes Bill a great deal - and you can bet he would be the first to deny it. But we know Better!! Thanks Bill.

Joe ColemanEric CrutcherKeith DuckettHarry PorterKeith SinclairDave MoteAndy PatonSue ParrottBill LawsBill Couzens

Notice · page 9

FINANCIAL MATTERS

Ron Severn reported on his first year as Hon Treasurer at the September AGM. Last year we had been warned that because the Hall would be out of action for 6 months during the rebuild and because we would need to start repaying the loans in 1985 there would be little likelihood of a profit. Surprise therefore when we learned of a substantial surplus of £2,095 for the year. The signs for 1986 are even better with lettings near to capacity. Things really seem to be in good shape and in capable hands.

Ron Severn

NIKE 25 x 1 MILE RELAY

Nike Blackheath H[illegible] 25 x 1mile

22nd SEPTEMBER 1985 CRYSTAL PALACE NIKE 25 x 1 MILE RELAY

With 9 of our top runners missing from our A team it was surprising that we ran 2 seconds faster than last year (when we won) but this time only finished 4th out of 80 teams. What tremendous depth we have with so many fine young athletes coming into our 'A' teams. Also our Ladies finished a full team for the first time. Below you will see results of 4 teams, but in addition we had a Colt, Boys and Youths team but their results have gone astray.

Once again the Couzens and Pritchards took the brunt of organising and recording our efforts.

Report · page 10

INDIAN SUMMER

Leo Coy has recently returned from a business trip to India and while in Delhi was royaly entertained by Ranjit Bhatia. Ranjit is expecting another Belgravian in the near future when Bob Taylor travels to India in November on holiday.

Super Vet Ranjit moved into the area of ultra long distance competition recently when he competed in the 250km race from Agra to Delhi. The race started at 7am on October 4th 1985 and 40km was covered before lunch with a further 25km in the afternoon. In the following days they covered 55km, 55km,65km, finishing with a short sprint of 15km into Delhi. Out of 256 starters over 200 finished the course and the event attracted Nationwide T.V. coverage.

Leo CoyRanjit BhatiaBob Taylor

Report · page 11

The 1985 London Marathon saw a large contingent of Belgravians lining up in support of The British Diabetic Association. The effort was led by Tony Grant, himself a diabetic who manages a pretty gruelling training schedule. The final total collected for this worthwhile cause was £2,800 and in June a mammoth sized cheque was handed to Dickie Henderson by Tony amid appropriate publicity. Well done everyone.

Tony GrantDickie Henderson

Notice · page 11

SPONSORSHIPS

Our highspeed postman Peter Gilbey travelled down to South Wales at the end of August and surprised the local opposition by winning the Torfean half-marathon by nearly 2 minutes in the excellent time of 66.11. As a result of this fine win he has been invited to become a member of the Reebok sponsored Road Squad.

Also now in the Reebok stable is Mark Sinclair following his sub 9 minute 3000S/C this summer.Several of our members also enjoy the support of major sports equipment suppliers. The current roundup is:-

BROOKS - Stuart Paton, Gary Staines. NIKE - Marcus Adams, John Gladwin, Philip Goedluck, Oliver Foote, John Regis. Reebok REEBOK - Peter Gilbey, Mark Sinclair.

Notice · page 11

MEMBERS HONOURED

At the September AGM Leo Coy and John Bicourt were elected Life Members of Belgrave while Ian Horton and John Bromley were elected Vice Presidents.

Column · page 12

WALKING

Carl Lawton

One of the advantages of being a member of a strong section is that even if you are a 'good' club man' usually finishing in 7th or 8th in the team there is always the chance that you may get the odd 4th spot when some cannot turn out and still get in the prizes. With a weak section you may always be in the team but the team is not good enough to gain prizes. With this in mind a glance through the results will show how many have been in the scoring teams during the winter months, especially the London 10 miles.

We still, however, must endeavour to encourage more people, not just to come into the sport but to stay in. The wastage of 'talent' is something we should all be concerned about, there is at the moment a gap in the 20-30 age group. Why else do you see a lot of the same faces heading fields that were there 10 years ago?

Meanwhile to the present. A good winter, but so was last year. Wins at Cambridge, Pewsey Cup, Belgrave and Imber Court, generally with good packing and never our best team out. Graham Seatter finished off his year back with us turning out when he could and showing Murray Day the ropes. During the year Graham collected two National medals and an Inter Counties team medal, not a bad achievement for someone who had given up walking for the last three years. Richard Dorman had his good raves, not as good as he had hoped but he is still a 'youngster', and his trips down from Nottingham for races should be an example to us all. Paul Warburton, back from his years sabatical in America, wasted little time in getting back into team placings, although the change in climate here did not suit him. Richard Tanner has almost returned to his standard of 7 years ago, not bad for someone who did not expect to take up walking again. John Newnham has not been at his best for a while but has still collected the odd team prize. Which brings me to the 'youngsters', Neil Foster, 3rd in the SCAAA 10kms and 2nd in the Club 10m, Michael Feighan (likened in style to Stan Vickers, but not quite the speed yet) and Murray Day. There is little one needs to say of Murray. Results say all that. And there is Stephen Marshall too, who, having had his work commitments reduced should be back in the spotlight. John Hall has had to lie low for a while due to varicose vein problems but is now back on the road.

Walking reports by Carl Lawton

Carl LawtonGraham SeatterMurray DayRichard DormanPaul WarburtonRichard TannerJohn NewnhamNeil FosterMichael FeighanStan VickersStephen MarshallJohn Hall

Results · page 14

BELGRAVE OPEN 7

Carl Lawton

24th NOVEMBER 1984 WIMBLEDON BELGRAVE OPEN 7

As always for this event I have to express appreciation for those who donated to the prize fund and thus more than doubling monies received from entry fees. To enable this event to continue unsponsored donations are the only way, but despite the prizes (over £200 and entry fee only 50p) we only had a field of under 100 and not a lot of 'retired' members helping with marshalling etc. But despite the moans and groans we still won the team, with Murray Day coming to life after a quiet Enfield for 4th place. Individual winner was Phil Vesty, winner two years ago, although not so fast. As it was it was fortunate for us that Leicester did not bring a strong 4th man as they had 3 in 6.

1.P.Vesty (Leics) 51.12; 2.M.Easton 51.34; 3.J.Ball (Soton) 52.05; 4.M.Day 52.31; 8.R.Dorman 54.18; 12.C.Lawton 55.10; 13.G.Seatter 55.12; 15.R.Tanner 55.47; 19.J.Hall 56.35; 27.J.Llewellin 58.14; 31.J.Newnham 58.53; 36.M.Feighan 60.17; 48.R.Middleton 62.40; 71.D.Sharpe 69.00; 74.J.Keown 69.28; 80.J.Morris 71.42; 85.G.Beecham 73.47; Team: 1.BELGRAVE 'A' 37; 2.Steyning 62; 3.Surrey W.C. 64; 10.BELGRAVE 'B' 203.

Results · page 14

PEWSEY CUP 7m

Carl Lawton

1st DECEMBER 1984 CALNE PEWSEY CUP 7m

Our previous trip to Calne in October had been, to say the least, a disaster. So as to prove we may have been down but not out, we ventured west once more with a little apprehension. The Pewsey Cup is not quite such a big race as the Chippenham to Calne but it was to be a race where everyone was to be on his Best Behaviour. 6 men travelling, 2 teams entered, 3 per team, so we could not lose anyone by the wayside. As it turned out all went well ending up with 1st and 3rd, we could have been 1st and 2nd if we could have known the finishing order beforehand, but you can't win them all, and on top of that there was another good 'B' team still at home.

1.J.Ball (Soton) 49.41; 2.C.Maddocks (Dawlish) 50.18; 3.K.Baker (Weymouth) 51.31; 4.M.Day 51.47; 5.C.Lawton 52.19; 6.G.Seatter 52.42; 7.R.Tanner 53.24; 14.P.Warburton 55.10; 15.J.Newnham 55.20; Team: 1.BELGRAVE 'B' 18; 2.Steyning 30; 3.BELGRAVE 'B' 33.

GRAHAM RETURNS TO NZ

Carl Lawton

5th JANUARY 1985 BEXLEY CAMBRIDGE H. LEAGUE MEETING

This race was Graham Seatter's final 'fling' on English soil. As it was he virtually flew the flag on his own for Belgrave. It turned out to be one of the coldest days and probably Graham's coldest race, but he survived to take 2nd place.

In the girls events Gail Porter managed a personal best and Rachael Parrott a 3rd.

Results · page 15

LONDON 10M CHAMPIONSHIP

Carl Lawton

9th FEBRUARY 1985 BATTERSEA PARK LONDON 10M CHAMPIONSHIP

This almost proved to be a non-event. Snow during the previous night reduced the number of starters to an embarrassingly low number of 16. Whether it should have been held is debatable, but had it been cancelled then it would have been unjust to those who turned up, many of whom had travelled from the outer limits of the boundary. Any way it was held with teams reduced to 3 a side, enabling 3 teams to start, Belgrave were the only team to start 4, but within a mile Paul Warburron was on the side-lines, having discovered that some walkers cannot cope with slippery surface. So it was left to Richard Tanner to not only bring home the team but to win overall with John Hall 2nd and Doug Fotheringham 5th. (The latter both did times faster than in the Belgrave). So it was another win for Belgrave, much to the pleasure of the Wandsworth Mayor who presented the awards.

Commiserations to Richard Dorman who, having travelled down from Nottingham the previous evening, failed to make Battersea from Wimbledon due to train cancellations.

1.R.Tanner 79.41; 2.J.Hall 83.14; 3.N.Carmody (C.H.) 85.33; 5.D.Fotheringham 87.48. Team: 1.BELGRAVE 8; 2.Cambridge H. 25; 3.Ilford 30.

In the Ladies 5kms, again a reduced field, Gail Porter finished 4th in 29.46.

Paul WarburronRichard TannerJohn HallN. CarmodyDoug FotheringhamWandsworth MayorRichard DormanGail Porter

Results · page 15

OPEN 10 MILES

Carl Lawton

16th FEBRUARY 1985 BASINGSTOKE OPEN 10 MILES

This event was last held two years ago, but missed a year as no one was willing to organise it, however this year it was back but turn out was not very good, although the weather conditions may have deterred many. Fortunately the course used was virtually snow free and if more courses could be found that are as traffic free some of our races would not be having the problems that they are. Belgrave's turnout was not too hot, a complete change of team to that which won the London 10 miles, but this time with Steyning out in force we had little chance, but a late burst from Carl Lawton helped us into 2nd place with John's Newnham and Dunsford (the latter with an arm in plaster) pulling their weight.

1.J.Ball (Soton) 73.28; 4.C.Lawton 76.47; 9.J.Newnham 82.12; 20.J.Dunsford 90.59; Team: 1.Steyning 15; 3.BELGRAVE 30; 3.Dawlish 31.

Results · page 15

AROUND THE COUNTIES

Carl Lawton

23rd FEBRUARY 1985 COUNTY 10 MILE CHAMPIONSHIPS

There was a time when Belgrave had competitors in a multitude of County Championships, this year we were down to three. Turn out in the south also seemed to be down and in certain cases results makes one wonder where we are going. In the Essex the first three were all 'old Lags' who have been competing for 16 to 20 years. Surrey had two youngsters there, but Middlesex had only one newcomer. As with last year we took a first and a second team position but we must somehow encourage new competitors.

SURREY 10 MILES AT MONKS HILL 1.M.Easton (S.W.C.) 73.25; 2.G.Vale (S.W.C.) 76.45; 3.C.Lawton 78.54; 4.R.Tanner 79.33; 6.J.Newnham 83.12; 7.E.Crutcher 89.49; 9.J.Morris 99.14; 10.J.Keown 103.58. Team: 1.Surrey 18; 2.BELGRAVE 20.

POST OFFICE 20Km.

Carl Lawton

14th APRIL 1985 BOURNEMOUTH POST OFFICE 20Km.

This event put on by former Belgravian George Williams although did not attract the hoped for numbers did at least get 15 Belgrave finishers and 2nd and 3rd team positions.

Feature · page 19

DOUG FOTHERINGHAM'S SEASON

Carl Lawton

Doug Fotheringham, one of Belgrave's Super Vets has undoubtedly had one of his best seasons, in various races Doug has collected winning team medals, GLC, Southern 20 miles, Southern 20 Kms and Track Competition. In addition to this he won two Silver medals in the European Championships in Rome and as a result of the Veterans A.C. 5 mile meetings at Battersea during the summer, came away as overall Grand Prise winner.

Doug Fotheringham

Photograph · page 19

First of the Last.

First of the Last. The abolition in 1986 of the G.L.C. meant that this years meeting held at West London Stadium on 31st August represented the last chance to win a G.L.C. title. John Hall ensured a place in the record books when he won the 3000m walk in 13.09.2 from P.Hannell of Surrey Walking Club 13.21.8 and N.Carmody of Cambridge Harriers. G.L.C. Photo.

Report · page 20

YOUNG ATHLETES

IAN HORTON

Last year I referred to the fact that we had completed the third term of a five year plan. I can now unveil the plan launched in 1981 in the knowledge that the boys have achieved that target this winter "to be regarded as the most successful Y.A. Club in Surrey". In 1981 this was ambitious as we had only 6 cross country runners in all age groups! But the most satisfying aspect is that 5 are still running and one of those has won an English Vest.

A glance at the following will confirm the outstanding results recorded in the three leagues in all age groups. Then the perfect peak culminating in the Surrey Championships when we took Silver, Bronze and Gold Team Medals respectively, to confirm the best overall performance by any Surrey Club. Incidentally 'Belgrave Historians' have said this is the best result in the history of Belgrave.

The athletes still credit their collective success to the Mars Bars, but all credit for this effort must go to the boys after many years of hard work and they are duly recognised in their respective sections. At the same time I know the boys would also wish to thank the increasing band of supporters, braving all weather conditions to make timely appearances around the course which strengthened everybodies resolve when the going got really tough. Also thanks to those who helped organise, transport and carry out co-operative duties. Not least appreciation to Rosemary for 100% attendance, recognised as the number pinning, track suit hanger, Mars Bar Lady.

Thanks to every body, above all to the boys who made this season the most enjoyable yet. And for the future - readers can be confident we have amended our plan.

IAN HORTON

Ian HortonRosemary
See it on the printed page →
Results · page 20

MINI COLTS

The efforts of these youngsters force their own place of recognition. In preparation to receive the mantle from the successful Colts for next season there are no faint hearts in this squad. Battling on the often bleak Epsom Downs they took second place in the League. A special note of Babs Khan and Teddy Davies for their consistently high performances, also rapidly improving Reece Wilkins and Stewart Booth.

Welcome to Neil Webster and Ben Brown who clearly showed talent that will make next season exciting. Also a special mention to Ben Cooper who, as our youngest runner (8), closed the team at the Ranelagh Open to secure his first ever Team Medal and with three more years as a Mini-Colt certainly not the last.

Results · page 21

COLTS

Predictably the Colts had by far their best season with four of the squad representing their county in the Inter-Counties. Very early signs of their imminent success showed at the Collingwood Open when they finished a close 3rd to Woking and Shaftsbury. They went on to win the Downs League with Matthew Egerickx taking the individual title. Frankly it must be said there was little opposition to the Colts taking the Surrey League title for the third consecutive year. So to the best day in the Bels Y.A. history when at the Surrey Championships this squad ran out of their skins to take the Silver close behind Woking with all 4 scorers in top 15. They finished 5th in the Herne Hill League, which is generally recognised as one of the highest quality leagues in the south.

Congratulations to Matthew, Steven, Alexander, Aaron, Dan W., Robert, all gaining representative honours.

Results · page 23

BOYS

The Boys were expected to struggle as a consequence of virtually all the regular runners being in their first year. Their character was under-estimated as any concern proved unjustified by the results. Due mainly to some superb runs by Duncan Ward, consistently supported by David Ashford.

Gareth Allison also Dean Cunningham and the notable improvement of Nadir Khan and Saul Herbert. These combined to take the Downs League Title, eighth in the Herne Hill League and Bronze Medal in the Surrey Championships, with a superb individual second place by Duncan Ward (robbed of the title by some indifferent marshalling when he was sent off course while leading, but to go on to take 18th place in the Southern Counties. The boys closed their season with a clear victory in the Ranelagh Open. Welcome to Steve Webster and congratulations to Duncan, David, Kevin, Dean, Gavin, Nadir and Saul on gaining representative honours.

Results · page 25

YOUTHS

This squad reached the level of achievement expected of them this season. Coincidentally as with the boys shouldering the imbalance of being predominantly a 'first year team' (it was hardly noticeable!) Even so, I do not overlook the invaluable contribution John Booth made, especially early season. Welcome also to Graham Adams and Simon Maynard, too late to effectively strengthen the squad, but a glance at the December 8th Surrey League result showed what is possible. Good luck to these three next season as Juniors.

The most significant team result was stealing a highly contested Surrey Championship title with a fine individual 4th from Garry McGurren, backed by Matthew Kinane (flying in from Switzerland hours before), Simon Beaumont and Glynn Tiernan. Not to be outdone the 'B string' runners showed consistent form to nail the Bronze in the Herne Hill League, largely from the efforts of Matthew Smith, Adam Horton and Robert Mann. It was not surprising that the same trio together with Brian Carlton should also take 2nd place in the Ranelagh Open.

Congratulations to Matthew, Glyn, John, Simon and Adam gain representative honours, also Garry for gaining a National Vest for an International Catholic Schools Race.

New members · page 26

NEW MEMBERS WELCOMED

SIMON SMITH: Aged 18 yrs. D.O.B. 18.8.66.

Simons athletic interests encompass a number of disciplines and to encourage his wide base interest he came to Belgrave in December 1984 from Mitcham A.C.

Personal bests include a 2710 Pentathlon score: 1.75 HJ; 5.37 LJ; 33m JT; 37.5 2kg Discus; 2.05' 800; 4.30.6 1500; 10.37 3000 S/C. He trains and competes regularly over the country and has a Surrey County Youth Team Bronze from Road Relays.

The athletic energy demands being so high for simon it is most fortunate for his family that he is a 1st Commis Chef at the Royal Lancaster Hotel, Hyde Park a la carte Restaurant where he gets first class fare to match.

GRAHAM ADAMS: Aged 16 yrs. D.O.B. 10.6.68.

Graham is a young athlete of great potential with a range from 400H to Cross Country and Road. He joined Belgrave in December 1984, an ex Mitcham A.C. member. An excellent steeplechaser he won the Surrey Youth title in 1983 as a 14 year old. A favourite to retain the championship in 1984 he unfortunately had to miss the event, and the bulk of the track season, with a mystery virus condition effecting the blood.

We have to look back to 1983 and to early 1984 for his best track and country performances. These include placing, 4th as a first year youth, in the county event and an excellent 71st in the National at Newark.

1983 Personal bests of 2.06.5 800; 4.19.8 1500; 9.16.6 3000 mark his existing potential, and his success over the steeplechase barriers is matched with useful prowess over 400H where he has notched 61 sec.

SIMON MAYNARD: Aged 17 yrs. D.O.B. 8.12.67.

Following a move from Mitcham A.C. in December 1984 Simon almost immediately showed his worth with a 3rd placing in the Surrey County Youths X.C. Champs at a time when training, which started late in November, pointed to only 90% readyness. February and March saw him fully fit but frustrated over his ineligibility to enter major events, such as Southerns and Nationals, owing to a first claim rule that keeps him off the Belgrave score sheet until 4.9.85.

County vests at cross country and track, have been an annual bonus for a lad who has a superlative record in every age group, mini colt upwards. He is also a Steeplechaser of note with a County Youths Silver Medal in recognition.

Best performances have so far come on the country and over the barriers, but he hopes to soon improve his 1984 3000 p.b. of 9.07 and expects in 1985 to get close to 2 min. for 800 and 4 min. at 1500. The following Cross Country season will be welcomed, particularly the prospect of high class team competition with his new friends at Belgrave.

STEVEN WEBSTER: Aged 14 yrs. D.O.B. 27.5.70.

Steve joined Belgrave in November 1984, the youngest of four athletes moving over from Mitcham A.C.

A schoolboy at Wilsons he feels he is treading a familiar athletic path at a school that has produced the Paton brothers; Dave Swain (E & E); Aidrian Izatt (SLH) in recent years.

There has been some early success including a win at the I.A.C. Cross Country (Crystal Palace) 1982: The year in which he ranked 4th in the U.K. 1500 Colts list.

October 1983 saw him corner a small piece of athletic history when he became the youngest ever male recipient of a AAA Championship Gold medal. A member of the successful Mitcham A.C. team in the inaugural AAA Championship (at this age group) road relays at Sutton Park West Midlands, Steve entered the record books in some style recording the fastest anchor leg in the event, and 4th fastest overall.

1984 brought a sixth place in the SCAAA Boys 1500 final at Crystal Palace in a time of 4.20.63. He also posted a 9.19.3 3000 on a freezing late February evening at Crystal Palace.

Simon SmithGraham AdamsSimon MaynardBill LawsSteven WebsterPaton brothersDave SwainAidrian Izatt

Feature · page 28

Back in May Steven won a Boys race in Battersea Park organised by Capital Radio. The prize was a trip in the Company's Flying Eye early morning traffic jam spotter plane. The only problem was the starting time - 6.30am.

Anyway, it was too good to miss and as the prize included for a friend to go along also it was at 5.30 am that Steven and Simon Maynard set off with Jim Webster acting as chauffer. The destination was Levesden airport near Watford.

Gale force winds and torrential rain looked as though they would provide a bumpy flight and it was with mixed feelings that the lads received the news that there would be a delay in proceedings. And so it went on. The flight would have to be another day. So back to south London and for Simon it was time to prepare himself for his first Club Championship - the 5000m Senior Champs at Battersea Park. And what a consolation! Yes the day turned out fine for Simon for he beat all our Seniors to win the 5000 in a personal best time of 15.21.6.

StevenSimon MaynardJim Webster

Report · page 29

IAN GILLEY SETS EXTRAORDINARY COLTS RECORDS

When the last issue of the Belgravian went to print we were aware that some good performances from our younger members had not reached the editorial office but we didn't realise that the missing marks put up by South African born Ian Gilley, during his stay in this country last Summer, were quite so remarkable.

Well, Ian's father Rod has now provided us with a summary of Ian's English results. He competed in 17 colts events, all of which he won,2 youths events placing 2nd and 4th and 1 senior event, placing 3rd. His best performances amongst these were 100m-12.1, 200m-25.5, 400m-58.6 and Long Jump-5.08m - all of which established new club records. But apparently we hadn't seen anything, for, on his return to South Africa, Ian has recorded the following astonishing marks:- 9.90(80m) 12.0(100m) 18.04(150m) 24.6(200m) 56.6(400m) and 5.41(Long Jump)!!!

Ian's family plan to bring him to the U.K. during the Summer of 1986 to compete in the Boys Age Group when doubtless we shall see more records tumble to this exceptional young man.

Ian GilleyRod Gilley

Report · page 30

DUNLOP YOUNG ATHLETES LEAGUE

Jim & David Mote

This was another year when we promised so much as a team but eventually had to be content with outstandingly consistent individual performances. Once again we suffered because we were unable to cover all events and to a degree this was caused by lack of control by management. We needed more helpers both in the lead up to matches and in management of the three sections on the day. However, we hope we will learn by our mistakes and they will not be repeated next year. I have been asked to take charge of the Young Athletes Section again next year and would welcome help in contacting athletes and organising them at matches. We have a considerable depth of talent in our section and it is up to us to make the best use of it.

The season was to comprise of five matches, but in fact was reduced to four because the final meeting at Redhill was called off because the track was flooded.

The following are short reports covering the four matches, highlighting the outstanding performances from these meetings.

12th MAY 1985 BATTERSEA PARK MATCH 1

In preparing for the opening meeting we were full of confidence in our ability to crush all opposition but were dismayed to realise on the day that the Surrey Championships were to start on the following day and for obvious reasons many of our middle-distance runners declined to compete. As some of these athletes also double up on field events we found ourselves with more gaps in our team. Nevertheless we were able to give a good account of ourselves and scored 28 wins, 13 seconds, 8 thirds and 4 other places leaving 24 events not covered and a consequent minimum loss of 24 points.

Outstanding performances:

400H: A C.Gardner (Y) 64.1; & 100H: A 14.7; 400H: B J.Mole (Y) 64.5; & 100H: B 15.6; 100: A M.French (B) 12.5 & 200: A 25.6; 100: B J.French (B) 12.8; 200: B J.Smith (B) 26.2 & 400: A 57.5; 100: A G.Warr (Y) 11.5 & 200: A 23.3; 200: B J.Minette (Y) 24.3; 100: B M.Dooley (Y) 11.9; HJ: A M.Reece (Y) 1.90; B G.Allison (Y) 1.65 & SP: B 10.13; TJ: A F.Ogwirda (Y) 12.73; B J.Mole (Y) 12.28; 80H: A J.Coy (B) 17.7; HJ: B W.Olufunwu (B) 1.55; LJ: B B Freik (Y) 5.51; DT: A D.Cunningham (B) 19.90; JT: B S.Warr (Y) 30.74; Youths 4x100: 47.5; Boys 4x100: 52.0; Youths 4x400: 3:50.3.

Results: 1.Woking 202; 2.BELGRAVE 171; 3.Hastings 117; 4.Ashford 81.

2nd JUNE 1985 WORTHING MATCH 2

This was a match we might easily have won but were outnumbered rather than outraced by the eventual winners. This time 'A' level studies reduced our team and again we had to be content with second place. There were, however, two outstanding features in this match in that we won all but three track races A and B in the Boys and Youths teams, but missed out where we had no competitors and Festus Ogwuda cleared 14.17 metres in the Youths Triple Jump to win the Dunlop Award for the outstanding feat in the match. This time we gained 33 wins, 18 seconds, 5 thirds and 6 other places, but again had 14 events not covered.

Outstanding performances:

PV: A S.Lambert (B) 1.80; 400H:A J.Mole (Y) 64.7, 100H: B 16.5 & TJ: B 12.95; 100m: A M.French (B) 13.1 & 200m: A 26.3; 100m: B J.French (B)13.2, 200m: B 26.4 & JT: A 27.56; 100m: A G.Warr (Y) 12.1 & 200m: A 24.9; 100m: B J.Minett (Y) 12.2 & 200m: B 24.2; TJ: A F.Ogwuda (Y) 14.17 & SP: A 13.01; 1500m: A K.Beisty (B) 4:49.6; SP: B M.Dooley (Y) 9.42; 400m: A J.Smith (B) 60.3; 400m: B W.Olufunwa (B) 62.6 & HJ: A 1.65; 400m: B B.Zreik (Y) 58.7 & LJ: B 5.65; 3000m: A G.McGurran (Y) 9.39.0 & 1500 S/C: A 4:55.6; 800m: A D.Cunningham (B) 2:18.0 & HJ: B 1.55; 800m: B G.Webb (B) 2:27.6; 3000m: A D.Ashford (B) 10:29.7; 3000m: B J.Coy (B) 11:43.6; Youths 4x100m: 47.2 & 4x400m: 4:07.8; Boys 4x100m: 50.9 & 4x400m: 4:09.8.

Results: 1.East Grinstead 257; 2.BELGRAVE 202; 3.Crowborugh 79; 4.Dorking 44.

30th JUNE 1985 EWELL COURT MATCH 3

This was our saddest day when our team already depleted by holidays was further hit when the vehicle bringing a number of athletes to the meeting, was delayed in traffic and missed the opening events. We still had our fair share of successes and were much heartened at the end of the afternoon when the Youths 4x100m Relay team screamed round the track to destroy all opposition in the remarkable time of 44.9. On the day we achieved 27 wins, 10 seconds, 12 thirds and 4 other places with 19 events not covered.

Outstanding performances:

100H: B J.Mole (Y) 15.5 & 400mH: A 62.7, & TJ: A 12.49; 100H: A C.Gardner (Y) 14.6; 100m: A M.French (B) 12.1, 200m:26.5; 400m: B 66.3; 100m: B J.French (B) 12.9 & 200m: B 26.5; 200m: A G.Warr (Y) 24.2 & 100m: A 11.7; 200m: B J.Minetti (Y) 23.9, 100m: B 12.0 & JT: B 29.32, 80H: A W.Olufanwa (B) 14.0, 1500m: A M.Kinane (Y) 4:20.9; 800m: A G.Tiernan (Y) 2:00.6; LJ: A M.Dooley (Y) 6.01; LJ: B S.Sbicca (Y) 5.37 & TJ: 11.04; DT: F.Ogwuda (Y) 31.26; SP: B S.Warr (Y) 7.98, LJ: B R.Affleck (B) 4.39; Youths 4x100m: 44.9 & 4x400m: 3:41.5; Boys 4x100m: 50.0 & 4x400m: 4:05.3.

Results: 1.Bexley 226; 2.Herne Hill 178; 3.BELGRAVE 176; 4.Sittingbourne 44.

7th JULY 1985 REDHILL MATCH 4

This meeting clashed with the London District meeting at Crystal Palace and apart from Matthew Kinane who was returning to competition after an absence for studies, robbed us of the entire Youths track team from 100m to 1500m. We were of course delighted that so many of our athletes were honoured by selection for their Districts, but it played havoc with our League team. Not downhearted the rest of the section competed with commendable spirit, doubling up wherever possible and achieving a very good result in the face of adversity. Special mention must be made of Festus Ogwuda who had competed in the morning session of the competitions at Crystal Palace and then made his own way to Redhill and arrived in time to add his considerable talent to the efforts of our depleted team. Nevertheless it was sad at the end of the day to find that we (who boast the fastest Youths 4x100 Relay Team in the South of England) had insufficient athletes available to enter a team in this event. Our achievements were 20 wins, 13 seconds, 8 thirds and 2 other places. We had no competitor in 34 events.

Outstanding performances:

4th JULY 1985 BATTERSEA PARK H.A.C. MEMORIAL TROPHY

After our sad reports about depleted team it is a pleasure to report the turn-out of Boys and Youths for this competition. THAMESIDE LEAGUE

This very popular competition again gave our Boys & Colts plenty to aim for. As a team we did not have much success, but individually the athletes competed with great spirit and many surprised themselves with their achievements in events where they had not hitherto considered themselves accomplished. It was interesting to see middle distance runners hurdling, sprinters jumping, and nearly everyone having a try at the field events at one time or another.

In summing up, having already mentioned the exploits of Festus Ogwuda. Mention must also be made of our Youths first choice sprint squad of Gavin Warr, Joe Minetti and Mark Dooley who competed whenever possible and were never beaten in the Young Athletes League sprints. When joined by Clive Gardner who is primarily a hurdler, but is also a class sprinter, they made up the relay squad that won the Surrey AAA, Youth 4x100m and ran the remarkable 44.9 at Ewell Court. Julian Mole also competed unstintingly providing valuable back up to Clive in the hurdles and Festus in the triple jump and taking over the 'A' competition when they were not available. Julian never missed a Young Athletes match and won every hurdles race and triple jump competition in which he competed 'A' and 'B'.

Finally, I will commend one boy who competed in every match - Young Athletes and Thameside Leagues - always completed three events and stood by for the relay if required. I of course refer to our middle distance hammer and discus throwing hurdler - John Coy.

Well done all of you, we look forward to another exciting season in 1986.

Jim & David Mote

Report · page 35

UNIQUE RECORD WIN

10th NOVEMBER 1984 WINDSOR SOUTH OF THAMES - JUNIOR

The South of the Thames 'Junior' Cross Country Championship is a senior event with barring conditions. Of our top men Pete Gilbey and Stuart Paton were not eligible. With Gary Staines injured we still felt we had enough fire power to worry the opposition. And so it turned out. Although the 5½ mile course was wet the stoney paths helped the faster track men to a good start and after 2 miles the 250 strong field was led by 4 Belgravians - Oliver Foote, Mark Sinclair, John Gladwin and Dave North, with Andy North and Francis Ward not far behind. Oliver opened up a gap from John with about 1 mile to go and won by 8 seconds followed by John, Mark in 6th and Dave 7th. The winning team's point score of 16 was the lowest ever beating the 19 pts record set by Belgrave in 1945 and the lads are now barred from this race. But the backing team could well repeat the victory next year with Andy North finishing a fine 13th and Francis Ward in 28th place. So a great day for Belgrave and Oliver having the satisfaction of adding his name to a cup with such greats as Chris Chataway (1949), Tim Johnson (1959), Dave Holt (1964), Pete Standing (1968), Dave Clarke (1977) and Roger Hackney (1978).

Results: 1.O.Foote 28.08; 2.J.Gladwin 28.16; 3.J.Starling (Bournemouth) 28.24; 4.G.Russell (Eps. & E) 28.27; 5.C.Tall (Basingstoke) 28.33; 6.M.Sinclair 28.44; 7.D.North 28.34 ... Other Bels: 13.A.North 29.04; 28.F.Ward 29.41; 45.M.Lake 30.09; 55.L.Lyons 30.32; 103.A.Gayton 31.50; 113.C.Millbank 32.07; 120.K.Francis 32.16; 190.M.Bizio 34.49; 201.J.Rayner 35.59.

5th JANUARY 1985 BROCKWELL PARK SURREY COUNTY CHAMPS.

Richard Partridge and Bob Treadwell had their own private battle for first. In the Juniors Andy North almost ran up to the form he had displayed in the Surrey league as he finished 5th to get a Surrey vest. Again the Juniors got 4th team and with a bit of luck could have got Silver.

Report · page 37

INTER COUNTIES

Two weeks after the Surreys our County representatives travelled to Braunceston Park Leicester to contest the Inter-Counties. In the Senior race a few overseas competitors added a bit of spice while Steve Cram was running to a fine 6th place to confirm his new winter training programme. Oliver Foote finished 46th in 37.07 but not finding the snow and ice to his liking. In the Junior race Andy North proved he should have had a Surrey medal as he finished in 55th place with 33.27 to be the second Surrey Junior man home. In the Youth race Simon Maynard was well in the 20's but faded to 45th in 20.31 as his lack of winter preparation showed. But what a promising young man he looks.

Steve CramOliver FooteAndy NorthSimon Maynard

Report · page 37

OLIVER AND ANDY EXCEL

3rd MARCH 1985 MILTON KEYNES NATIONAL C.C. CHAMPIONSHIP

It was cold, wet and unbelievably muddy at Milton Keynes as nearly 2000 Senior runners tackled the toughest race of the winter season. Each season the field gets bigger and this year saw 194 teams completing the course with Belgrave managing 45th place. Mike Hayes again gave an indication that he is over illness as he moved steadily through while Dave North who had succumbed to 'flu the week before battled gamely through to finish. Martin Lake too ran brilliantly on a reported 30 miles a week training schedule. Leo Lyons missed the start! But pride of place went again to Oliver Foote. Not known to like muddy courses he had a battle royal with Karl Harrison (who was to feature so well in 10k Road Races in the summer) with Karl finishing in 56th place and Oliver in 59th.

And as for the Juniors - what can you say of Andy North's run. While others sank to their knees Andy floated across the mud and reminded many of us who were fortunate to see his run of another great North of some 30 years earlier! His 14th in the Southern should have equated to around 60th in the National (indeed local rival Mark Slowikowski finished 65th) but Andy seems to do better the harder the race. And so it was 20th place and some well known scalps to his credit.

Results:

Senior: 1.D.Lewis (Rossendale) 44.30; 2.D.Clarke (HW) 44.36; 3.D.Murphy (Liverpool) 44.50; 17.B.Ford (AFD) 46.40; 18.J.Richards (Cornwall) 46.47; 25.A.Holden (Tipton) 47.02; 30.R.Partridge (AFD) 47.18; Bels: 59.O.Foote 48.14; 207.M.Hayes, 265.M.Lake 448. Report · page 39

CAMBRIDGE H ROAD RELAY

17th MARCH 1985 BEXLEY CAMBRIDGE H ROAD RELAY

Mike Hayes was the only member of last year's bronze medal team to return to Bexley and it is a measure of our increasing strength that we again came third narrowly failing to pip Aldershot for silver. Gary Staines was eager to run leg 1 and it soon became clear that he is moving into excellent form as at 2 miles he stretched away to record 15.42 for this near 3½ mile course and in so doing ran the fastest time of the day. Andy North took over 16 seconds in the lead and ran 32 seconds faster than last year (he was in the 'B' team then) to hand over still 14 seconds clear. Junior, John Warren, showed that he has now overcome illness and injury to record an excellent 16.53 and kept us in contention as we slipped to 3rd place. Steve Smullen, who a week earlier had run a 1.57 800m, slipped further back to 4th, but Stuart Paton pulled us back with a useful 16.27. As Mike Hayes took off 51 seconds behind the second placed Aldershot man he waited to see if he would be caught by the clan following Windsor and Invicta men. Imagine the surprise as he came into view of the finish breathing down the neck of the Aldershot man. Alas his early effort proved too much. But we had some first class performances to look back on. Particularly noteworthy was Chris Millbank's 'B' team run following his previous days win in the 5¼.

'A' Team:1.G.Staines 15.42; A.North (1) 16.36; J.Warren (3) 16.53; S.Smullen (4) 17.22; S.Paton (3) 16.27; M.Hayes (3) 16.12;

'B' Team: L.Lyons (21) 18.09; C.Millbank (19) 17.13, L.Coy (17) 18.25; D.Anderson (19) 18.31, M.Bizio (15) 17.56, DNF.

14th APRIL 1985 WIMBLEDON SOUTHERN 12 STAGE RELAY

Last minute efforts by Clive Shippen helped prevent the Southern 12 Stage Relay being switched by the SCAAA's to the unpopular Thamesmead site. Feature · page 40

AND WHERE WAS THE CROSS COUNTRY CAPTAIN THIS WINTER ?

Don Anderson

Having not run for most of 1984, due to injury, I decided something positive was needed to "Bump start" my body back into action, so I chose to go trekking in the Himalayan mountains. This would be of little benefit for the road or track, but would be a good reminder of what hard work and endurance was all about.

So off I set from Pakhora, a village in the valley, with tent, sleeping bag and back-pack weighing 26 kilograms, to Annapurna base camp, some 17,000 feet above sea level. Food and chlorinated water were essential items, as the area was rife with hepatitis, spinal meningitis and amoebic dysentery, not to mention sickness and diarrhoea; after all I was here to gain fitness not lose it.

The first two days were a pleasant trek up to 3,000 feet, but the map I used was very inaccurate. It led me up the creek, so I scaled the mountainside in order to reach the village of Kanre, some 5,400 feet above sea level, to put me back on the right course. After that it was steady trekking to Tikedhunga. The paths were well used by donkey trains - the only means of transporting supplies to outlying villages but they carved the paths up making the going uneven and difficult.

Back in the Khala valley it was nice to stop for a bath in the glacier fed river. It was cold, but certainly refreshed the parts that weren't often reached.

Pressing on to the village of Shorepani, 9,300 feet above sea level, the snow settled on the tent at night and made the inside freezing. On to the village of Shanrung, which is above 10,000 feet. The view was breathtaking with the morning sun radiating the snow capped Annapurna peak and Machapuckhie (fish tail) mountains like forbidding gods. The next was the village of Chomrong, still climbing. By this time I was running short of time, so I decided to leave my kit at one of the lodges and run up to Annapurna base camp wearing only running kit. The view from the mountain was again breathtaking, and well worth the 5,000 mile trip.

The adventure was by no means over, for during my descent to Kathmandu, I was robbed of my money and passport. The British Embassy refused to help me and so I had to resort to my own initiative to get back to Delhi, India. So I borrowed a bicycle and cycled hundreds of miles relying solely on local hospitality to provide me with food and accommodation. After some wheeling and dealing I crossed the border into India. From there to Delhi I hitched a lift on the footplate of a steam train, where we had tea and chapaties cooked on the train's boiler.

The British High Commission in Delhi were more sympathetic to my cause and issued me with an emergency passport and lent me some cash whilst I waited for my flight to London.

It was a very interesting trip, but in future, I shall limit my altitude training to Box Hill!

Don Anderson

Don Anderson

Records · page 41

ALL TIME TOP 100 OVER THE 5 3/4

Steve Williams

It is now over 10 years since the Belgravian reproduced the top 100 performances over our 5 3/4 mile road course. And there is good reason why this particular bunch of statistics has laid dormant for so long - there have been hardly any runs inside the 100th best mark of 10 years ago! In fact, just one run apiece from Messrs O'Hara, Weller, Stow, Duncan, Bicourt and Foote. So in case you didn't realise what represents a good run round the course, here is the list updated - perhaps we can anticipate a greater number of changes in the coming decade! Still shown are runs from the 50's from Charlie Walker, Pat Newell and Ron Linstead and, as you would expect, Laurie O'Hara retains the largest single share of the list with 15 runs. For your information, and to set a target for some of you, another list, showing the all time top 100 individuals, has been produced in preparation for publication in our centenary year. This includes all your old favourites - Tom Carter, Arthur Penny and Ted Stimpson to name a few - and the present 100th spot clocks at 29.50 - so get cracking if you're hovering on the 30 minute mark, you've only four races left.

* 2nd claim at the time

STEVE WILLIAMS

Report · page 42

29TH WELLINGTON INTERN. RELAY

31st MARCH 1985 OSTEND 29TH WELLINGTON INTERN. RELAY

For a variety of reasons only Dave Mote, Guy Sagar, Mark Sinclair and Derrick Williams of last years winning 12 made the return journey for this years International Relay held on the Wellington race-course at Ostend. We took the opportunity to "blood" the youngsters and new members. With Oliver Foote and Mike Hayes adding experience over the longer legs and superb runs from Steve Smullen and Mark Sinclair we finished in bronze position. A truly splendid performance in this elite company with Aldershot and Blackheath left trailing. Every run was impressive and it was great to see our new 400m squadies - Mike Bazire and Will Willson doing so well. Dave Mote looked as though he's over injury and attacked into gale force winds. Guy Sagar produced his usual gutty run- while Junior, Simon Maynard made a fine debut at this level of competition.

The team was grateful for the vociferous encouragement from 13 loyal supporters who were rewarded with fine sunshine, but gale force winds which managed to suit a Hovercraft at Dover!! And thanks to Don Anderson. Forced to work nights he travelled over on the day of the race just to cheer on the team, and then rushed straight back well satisfied with our efforts.

TRACK AND FIELD

An eventful 12 months with many individual and team successes plus a considerable number of personal best performances. Almost all of our senior Club Records were under threat and several fell; with John Regis taking Ernest's 200m time, John Gladwin bettering his own 800m time, Ken O extended his discus record and Gary Staines became our first sub 4 minute miler. Ernest became our first AAA's track champ winning an exciting 100m in a year when he has given unstinting support to our British League Side. If you're of an excitable nature skip this Section !

Reports by:- Bob Bridges, Leo Coy, Steve Williams and Bill Laws.

Report · page 43

INDOOR ROUND-UP - GREAT SPRINTING FROM ERNEST OBENG AND JOHN REGIS

Steve Williams

With the middle distance boys giving the indoor season a miss, it was mainly the sprinters who kept the Belgrave name to the fore on the boards in 1985.

Ernest Obeng began what must be his most significant indoor campaign on 12 January at the Cosford Games when he sprinted to a fine p.b. over 60m - 6.68 - just shaded by the eventual European Champion, Mike McFarlane but ahead of some useful domestic competition in Watson, Asquith and Sharp. Phil Lovell was another Belgravian in 2nd place with a 4.70 pole vault behind Jeff Gutteridge.

Two weeks later, Ernest lined up again at Cosford to defend his AAA's title. Amongst those alongside him were McFarlane again and Desruelles of Belgium and although he was away as well as these two, he lost out in the pick up from 30 metres and had to be content with the bronze medal. Nevertheless his time of 6.71 was still faster than he had run prior to this season. Mike Alleyne had also travelled up for this event and recorded 7.18 in the heats. Unfortunately, Phil Lovell no heighted in the vault this time.

Ernest then departed for a short visit to the continent and took advantage of the better indoor surface at Sindelfingen, W.G., where, on 1st February, he won the 60m event in a fabulous new club record of 6.63 which is one of the fastest times ever in the World - the third fastest by a Commonwealth athlete before McFarlane's European run! Two days later Ernie backed this up with a 6.65 at Karlsruhe. Great sprinting!

The Juniors next took the stage at Cosford on 17 March at the AAA Under 20 Championships and more medals fell to the claret and gold as our new sprinter, John Regis, demonstrated some real quality with a tremendous victory at 200m in a CBP of 21.93, which is some sprinting round the tight bends of an indoor track. In fact, this run placed John as the 8th fastest ever UK indoor performer!

John had previously placed 2nd in the 60m championship in 6.90 and although 200m seems his specialist distance we can anticipate some good things from him over 100m as well. Phil Goedluck, in his first year as a Junior recorded 7.07 and 22.63 in the heats, which made him the fastest 17 year old, and Phil can look forward to two more attempts at these titles!

At the other end of the age range, our 'new' Vet, Snowy Brooks, was in good form at the British Veterans Championships held at Cosford on 23 March. Snowy won the 60mH in 9.3 and the Long Jump in a new UK Best performance of 6.14. He also placed 5th in the 60m in 7.8 (7.7ht) and recorded 25.4 in the heats of the 200m.

Steve Williams

Report · page 44

EARLY BIRDS

Following several low key performances last summer it seemed as though Michael Allayne was approaching the 1985 Track and Field season with renewed enthusiasm. We first spotted his 7.18 sec. 60m effort in the 1985 AAA's Cosford Indoor Champs. This was soon followed by an excellent 35.1 300m at an open meeting at Dartford. In March he ran a p.b. 49.5 sec 400m at an open meeting at the Crystal Palace and it is in this event that his many Belgrave fans believe he has the greatest potential. A sub 48.0 this season is a real possibility.

Other impressive early season results are to hand. Several Belgravians have notched up p.b's which indicate a fine year ahead for many of our established Stars. On the 20th April at Battersea Park, Mark Sinclair ran his first 2000 S/C and won in 5.54.8 closely followed by Mike Hayes in 5.57.7. Four days later Mike won a Southern Counties Match 3000 S/C at Crystal Palace with a huge p.b. in 9.03.9 while the following Saturday Mark won the Surrey 3000 S/C in 9.12.0 at the Croydon Arena 'dust bowl'. Oliver Foote and David North - our mainstays for the British League 5000m - confirmed early season form with p.b's of 8.17.3 and 8.26.6 at the Southern Counties match while a week later in a graded meeting Mark Sinclair also ran a p.b. 3000 in 8.20.1.

While this was going on 16 year old Graham Adams ran 60.7 for the 400H - again a big p.b. while 20 year old John Warren lowered his 3000 p.b. to 8.37.2.

Michael AllayneMark SinclairMike HayesOliver FooteDavid NorthGraham AdamsJohn Warren

Report · page 44

NIKE BLACKHEATH H 25 x 1 MILE - 1984

The official results of the 1984 25 x 1 Mile Relay were a long time coming and with good reason. After extensive debate between Blackheath and sponsors Nike, Sale were disqualified from 1st spot as a result of some runners competing without Club vests while others were paced. As a result Belgrave repeated their 1983 victory and the winning team each received a pair of Nike Transit trainers while Gary Staines, who recorded the days fastest time with 4.06 also won a pair of spikes. With so many up and coming youngsters it should take 4.35 to make the team this year. The date is Sunday, September 22nd at Crystal Palace and a UK record by Belgrave is on the cards if everyone turns out.

Gary Staines
See it on the printed page →
Report · page 44

SOUTHERN LEAGUE ROUNDUP

First of all, I would like to thank all those who turned out in the Southern League this season, all 68 of them ! I think this proves that we are giving as many people as possible competition at this level. With the strong likelihood of a 'C' team next season there is room for even more. So never think you are not good enough. We try to fit every one in somewhere and don't forget there are non scoring races at 200m and 1500m at every meeting.

A look through the results will show who did what and where and quite a few of the 'A' team will be looking over their shoulders at the quality of some of them. Those that stand out are the two club records that were set; Andy North's junior 5,000 record of 14:55:8 at Battersea on 25th May and Glynn Tieoran's 1500 youth record of 4:02:8 also at Battersea on 3rd August. There were also a host of p.b.'s set throughout the season.

So with a few more people willing to travel to away matches and prepared to "race" on cinder, we may have two teams in their respective premier divisions in our centenary year.

Andy NorthGlynn Tieoran

Report · page 49

SIMON MAYNARD'S FIRST TITLE

8th MAY 1985 BATTERSEA PARK CLUB CHAMPIONSHIP

Our first Club Championship of the season coincided with the 40th Anniversary of VE day and for 17 year old Simon Maynard his 5000m debut turned into a dream. Showing great maturity he sat on the leaders throughout taking the lead only in the finishing straight as he eventually overcame the strength of Martin Lake while the first five home all recorded p.b's.

The supporting events were all well attended. In the 800 John Booth looked impressive as he followed Steve Smullen through the bell in 56 secs and also recorded a p.b. In the throws Ken, Tony Featherstone and Mike Small came together for the first time confirming our growing strength in the field events.

Report · page 49

SURREY COUNTY CHAMPIONSHIPS (PART 2)

13-18th MAY 1985 MOTSPUR PARK SURREY COUNTY CHAMPIONSHIPS (PART 2)

With several members chosing not to compete we were nonetheless splendidly represented with a host of medals. Mark Sinclair failed by a whisker to win the 1500m where heats and final were run within 2 hours due to the earlier heats being washed out. Mark Shippen finally got it together in the pole vault. Derrick Williams showed some impressive speed and looks set for a best ever season. Oliver Foote too surprised with a fast last 800m in the 5000 being recorded at 57 secs for the final 400m as he gained a silver in a p.b. But pride of place must go to the Youths with Gavin Warr clocking a fine 23.1 in the 200m final and Glyn Tiernan breaking a 15 year old Championship Best Performance to win the Youths 1500m by a huge margin. (Historians may note that the previous record was held by none other than Belgrave's Bill Custin who ran 4:07.1 in 1970).

Youths: 100: 2.G.Warr 11.5; 3.M.Dooley 11.8; 200: 2.G.Warr 23.1; 3.M.Dooley 24.0; 1500: 1.G.Tiernan 4:05.3.

Report · page 50

BRITISH LEAGUE - DIVISION 3 RESULTS - MATCH 1

BRITISH LEAGUE - DIVISION 3 RESULTS

11th MAY 1985 ENFIELD MATCH 1

With last years visit to Enfield in mind - bad weather and a third place - we hoped that both would improve in this the first match in Division 3. Sadly, the weather was much worse, but our performances warmed everybody as we achieved a solid second place - having led Windsor by two points with one relay to go!

Bels had eight individual wins - Ernest, having been caught by St. Louis in the 100, won the 200, Stuart and Gary had fine double wins in the 800, Mark Sinclair outkicked the field to win the 1500 and he was backed up well by Steve Smullen, who won the 'B' event. Pete Gilbey won the 'B' S/C, having avoided the elbow to elbow tactics of Large (S & E) and Mike Hayes. Ken won the discus in very unfavourable conditions and our 4x100 team had a comfortable win. Mike Bazire and Tony Featherstone made their BAL debuts, in a very satisfactory start to the season.

Results:

Report · page 50

MATCH 2

1st JUNE 1985 WINDSOR MATCH 2

Glorious weather was the order of the day at Windsor and hopes were high of a repeat of last years fine win. Unfortunately Southampton and Windsor turned out very strong teams and we had to be content with third place. We again headed Windsor by two points with just the last relay left and they again managed to pip us at the post. Ernest, thought that he had nicked the 100 from Todd Bennett as they crossed the line together, but the S & E man was given the verdict. Gary almost caught the England Captain on the line in a fine 800 metres race. Stuart won the 'B' event and John Regis made his BAL debut by winning the 'B' 100, but unfortunately an injury kept him out of the short relay, despite this the 4x100 team equaled the Club Record. In the Long Jump Derrick Williams led the field with a good 7.15 metres and he also won the 'B' TJ. Ken again triumphed in the discus and almost sneaked a win in the shot. Will Willson, Dave Catling, Leo Lyons and Nigel Clarke competing in the BAL for the first time, all performed exceptionally well.

Results:

Report · page 51

MATCH 3

30th JUNE 1985 BRIGHTON MATCH 3

At the half way stage in the League, the promotion battle was between Windsor, Bels and Southampton while Enfield, Brighton and local rivals HHH all had to fight to avoid the drop. Brighton, the home club had never finished lower than second in all their previous home BAL Matches. Southampton were getting their act together following their win at Windsor and WSE were determined to keep their lead at the top of Div.III. It promised to be a great day and that is how it turned out. We were always in the first three throughout the afternoon and did not lead until very late. Our double wins in the 5000 (Olly and Mark both with p.b's) and the Pole Vault (Phil and Mark) pushed us into the lead that we did not lose although it was very close with just ½ a point between Southampton and ourselves after an exciting final relay. Enfield did us a favour by tying with Windsor and this resulted in the Bels heading Div.III after a memorable day. Individual performances that should be mentioned are Mark Lindsay-Bayley who filled in for Derrick Williams in the Triple after Derrick had injured himself in the first round of the Long Jump. Derrick was also a key member of the Relay team and Aham volunteered to run in his place and he also had a go at the High Jump! Andy Paton came along as a spectator and in borrowed gear ran a storming first leg in the Long Relay. Mike Bazire pushed himself to the limit as he gave all in both relays. With this tremendous team spirit we won a match where we could have easily finished third. The other heroes of the day were the throwers - they really excelled. Mike Small in his first BAL Match for us had a great afternoon. Ken threw the hammer for the first time and Tony Featherstone threw 46 metres in the discus, to get a p.b. by a large margin. Ernest broke Peter Radfords ground record in the 100 and left Todd Bennett for dead as he scorched to a fine win. MLB and Dave Sheldon ran p.b's in the Long Hurdles and Alan Benn made his debut in the BAL.

Results:
