Report · page 52

MATCH 4

10th AUGUST 1985 SOUTHWARK PARK MATCH 4

This last match was switched from Tooting and despite protests was held at Southwark Park. The afternoon got off to a stormy start as most of the vaulters refused to take part as the conditions were dangerous. The match followed the same pattern as before with the Bels never out of the first three and we took the lead with just a few events to go. It turned out to be a memorable day for the Club and its many supporters - they drowned the other teams supporters with their noise.

Our throwers were again magnificent - out of eight events they recorded 4 wins, 3 seconds and a third place. It was good to see Vincent Cornibert again and he certainly shook-up the Javelin competition. 16 year old Graham Adams came into the team at the last minute and ran really well to finish 3rd in the 'B' 800. Tom Taylor also made his BAL debut for us and had two splendid second places. Chris Cornwell equalled the Club Record and became our first 2.00 High Jumper in BAL Competition. Another performance that went unnoticed was that of Derrick Williams in the Triple Jump. Both he and Aham were struggling below their best when with one jump left he produced a magnificent 14.75 to completely change the points position for the event. Pride of place must go to the 4x100 Relay team who recorded a new Club and Div.3 League record with a spectacular 41.1 with a good second in the 4x400 Relay it rounded off a marvellous afternoon for the Club. Our winning points score of 256 was the highest of the 4 matches despite the absence of the pole vault result in which we normally amass around 20 points.

Results:

Letter · page 54

From Brian Pritchard

Brian Pritchard

LETTERS

From Brian Pritchard

I would like to congratulate the Club on their achievement of winning Division 3 of the British League.

Having followed our team very closely during the past five years, I believe our rise in consecutive seasons from Div.5 to Div.2 is quite remarkable considering the very high standard of the British League.

I would firstly like to congratulate the team on their superb achievement and in the way they have responded when asked to turn out for the Club, and secondly to thank Leo Coy and Bill Laws for the magnificent way they have managed the team, and I can not think of anyone else who could have done the job as well.

I look forward to next season with anticipation. I am sure I speak for all the supporters who have turned up at the meetings, in wishing the team, Leo and Bill the best of luck for next year.

Brian PritchardLeo CoyBill Laws

Letter · page 54

From Derrick Williams

Derrick Williams

As promised a few lines about my latest filming exploits. The saga starts one Friday morning, going about my usual days work when I received a 'phone call from Jim Alford who wanted to know if I was available for a few days, as they were in the process of making a series of videos for coaching consisting of all the events in track and field.

My involvement was slightly different in that with the other video they had an experienced athlete e.g. Seb Coe, David Hemery etc. to both demonstrate and talk about the techniques involved in their events, but I came into the story because for the long jump they had Lyn Davies who was quite able to coach, but because of a long-standing injury didn't feel able to run and jump. So a replacement was called for. Not being able to get an exact double they searched for the next best thing - a tall very handsome and capable athlete. But failure again left them with just one option ... me.

So my day began at seven thirty a.m. Taxi to wisk me off to the film location which was not L.A. as I had hoped, but sunny Watford. Arrived at around 8.45 a.m. and met everyone involved; the director Tony Maylam who made the "Super Milers", the film crew, Lyn Davies and the young athletes who were all still at school. The day began with Lyn breaking down the preparation for any session, warm up stretching, drills etc. Then with the aid of the youngsters he did the approach run, take off, movement through the air and landing. All the youngsters performed very well.

They were of different standards which was lucky as they showed common faults any beginner would have. By 3 o'clock they had done the maximum work their tired bodies would allow. So I was told to make ready for my entrance. I had been sitting to one side up to this point watching and learning all the little points Lyn Davies mentioned.

I was supposed to be the finished article, with the technique the youngsters should hope to one day emulate, so for the next three hours I was running flat out and jumping into a pit littered with camera men and equipment. They were filming with three different cameras so I was at it time after time after time and at around 6 o'clock I was wondering how much longer when those magic words sung out "Just one more take Derrick". I thought we'll have to make this a good one. So using my 'fabled speed" I sprinted to the board and launched myself high into the air and to the horror of the camera men landed less than six inches from the cameras sending sand over everyone. Thankfully they saw the funny side.

And so ended a memorable days work, very hard but also very rewarding, meeting Lyn Davies was an honour.

With best wishes to all.

Derrick WilliamsJim AlfordSeb CoeDavid HemeryLyn DaviesTony MaylamJoe Coleman
See it on the printed page →

ROSENHEIM LEAGUE 1985

Steve Williams

ROSENHEIM LEAGUE 1985 Compiled by Steve Williams

This year, the Seniors made the final for what I believe is only the second time and no doubt Mike Bazire (10.9/22.4) and Mike Small (49.82 Hammer) amongst others were pleased about that. As they were the two mainstays through the season, they deserved it. Croydon just pipped us in the final. Some highlights along the way were Dave North's 8.19.4 (17th Bels all-time) in Match 4, followed home by a string of other fine pb's, including brother Andy's 2nd best ever Bels Junior mark - also Jay Busk's 50.7 - and how about John Booth's 1.57.9 on that pit of a track at Croydon.(With Tooting available next year hopefully we won't have to go there more than once!). Amongst the Youths, Joe Minetti looks to be another sprint find. Non scorers are shown in brackets.

Column · page 57

THE STEVE WILLIAMS' ROUND-UP

Steve Williams

ROUND-UP OF THE BEST OF BELS INTERNATIONAL, CHAMPIONSHIP AND REPRESENTATIVE PERFORMANCES

As has become usual in recent years, we were well represented in the early SCAAA matches at Crystal Palace. On 24 April, Olly Foote (8.17.3) and Dave North (8.26.5) ran pb's over 3000m while Mike Hayes placed 2nd in the 'chase in 9.03.9, also a fine pb; Phil Lovell won the Pole Vault at 4.80 and Ken Onuaguluchi placed 2nd in the Discus with 51.34. On 1 May, Stu' Paton was 2nd over 800m in 1.53.5, Mark Sinclair 2nd in the Southern 'Standards' 3000m in a pb of 8.20.1, and Phil again won the vault with 4.80 and, this time, Ken, the Discus with 49.88.// AAA's representative vests were won by four Bels on 15 May at C/Palace where John Regis won the 200m in 21.8, Gary Staines was 4th over 1500m in 3.47.5, Mark Sinclair 6th in the 2km 'chase in 5.57.2 and Phil Lovell 3rd in the Vault again at 4.80.// On 21 May Stuart Paton was over in Norway where he ran what turned out to be a seasons best 1500m in 3.48.57.

* * *

UK Championships - Antrim - 25/26 May - JOHN REGIS shares 200m title:- A trip over the Irish Sea paid handsome dividends for John Regis, Gary Staines and Phil Lovell who all collected medals. John brought home Gold from his dead heat with Linford Christie in the 200m in a magnificent 21.59 (club jnr. rec.) despite carrying a hamstring problem, and backed this up with Silver in the 100m in 10.5, also a club jnr. rec. A feature of John's running was his remarkable finishing speed. Gary took Bronze in the 1500m in a typically gutsy run (3.44.22) and Phil found his consistency at 4.80 bringing Silver in the vault - a fine performance in miserable conditions. Also competing were Mike Hayes - 9.12.4 for 14th in the 'chase and Stu' Paton 3.54.17 in the 1500m heats.

* * *

At the Glasgow Games on 9 June, Ernie Obeng held off Sharp and Watson over 100m in 10.3 and, representing Loughbro' against AAA's, three days later, won at 100m in 10.45w and placed 2nd over 200m in 21.22w.

* * *

SCAAA Championships - C/Palace - 14/15 June - GARY STAINES wins 1500m:- Our highlight was undoubtedly Gary's win in the 1500m. It was his first major championship outdoors and his determined final 200m in a closing lap of 56.9 showed improved finishing. Well done Gary. Medals were also won by Stu' Paton - close to his best time over 800m but too far off the pace when the winner jumped the field; Phil Lovell, 3rd in the Pole Vault and Ken Onuaguluchi 2nd in the Discus - a medal in these championships is becoming a nice habit for these two. Results:100m D Williams 11.15ht 200m M Alleyne 22.47ht, P Lyttle 23.03ht 400m G Sagar 52.30ht 800m 3rd S Paton 1.49.03, 5th J Gladwin 1.50.35(1.49.85sf), N Clarke 1.55.39ht-pb 1500m 1st G Staines 3.42.89 5000m O Foote 14.53.26 3000mSc 12th M Hayes 9.14.54 PV 3rd P Lovell 4.80 LJ 8th D Williams 7.03 TJ 10th A Amadi 14.34 SP 6th K Onuaguluchi 14.89pb DT 2nd K Onuaguluchi 54.56.

* * *

On 21st June, JOHN REGIS became the latest Belgrave International when selected to run 200m against the USA at Birmingham. This was something of a firey baptism for an athlete still a junior and John should not be too unhappy with his 6th place (21.68) in company which included the Olympic Bronze medallist, the World Silver medallist and a 20.07 performer in the US team! *** GARY STAINES ran 3.44.77 in Poland on 24 June. *** On 30th June, STUART PATON was an International winner for Scotland against Ireland and Catalonia, using that great acceleration to swamp Paul Forbes amongst others in the 800m in 1.50.97.*** At the World Veterans Championships in Rome at the end of June, SNOWY BROOKS was a match for the best with a Silver Medal in the Long Jump (6.42) and Bronze in the Pentathlon (3475 pts).

* * *

Surrey v Army v Hants - Aldershot - 3 July: 3000mSc 5th M Sinclair 9.24.0; 110mH 3rd T Taylor 15.3, 5th M Lindsay-Bayley 15.4; PV 1st P Lovell 4.60; LJ 1st T Taylor 6.99; TJ 1st A Amadi 14.42; SP 3rd D Dawson 14.18, 4th P McKay 14.15.

* * *

English Schools Championships - Hull - 12/13 July:- Snr Boys 200m 2nd M Adams 21.9(21.6sf) 5th P Goedluck 22.3(22.1sf): Inter Boys 200m G Warr 23.0sf TJ F Ogwuda 13.57qual.

ERNEST OBENG AAA'S CHAMP.

AAA Championships - C/Pal - 13/14 July - ERNEST OBENG 100m CHAMPION:- Third in 1978, second in 1981 and now first in 1985, Ernest Obeng has become only the second Belgrave runner to win a Senior AAA title by taking the 100m championship this year (his only predecessor being Bill Nankeville who won the Mile in 1948). Coming comfortably through his heat and semi-final in 10.51 and 10.42 respectively, Ernie looked an obvious medal prospect, although the American 10.10 performer, Darwin Cook, who had already beaten Ernie in the Ulster Games, must have been favourite. And it was Cook who got the best start in the final with Ernie, suprisingly one of the last away. So with what is usually the best part of Ernie's race over, observers could have been forgiven for thinking our man would be run out of it - but, maintaining his form wonderfully through the last 20 metres in a storming finish, Ernie just edged ahead when it counted. Only 5/100ths covered the first 5 across the line but Ernie was in no doubt he had made it, celebrating with one arm raised aloft as he ran through. Well done Ernest! The full result for posterity:-1st E OBENG 10.44; 2nd D Cook(USA) 10.45; 3rd Imoh(Nig) 10.46; 4th L Asquith 10.49; 5th B Watson 10.56; 6th D Reid 10.72 (McFarlane 10.46 disq.,Christie dnf) The wind was 1.4m/s against./// Our other top men in action were John Gladwin - first Briton home in the 800m in 5th place with 1.47.08, the start of a great series of runs from him, lasting to the end of the season; Gary Staines - a pb of 3.41.48 in qualifying for the 1500m final where he placed 10th in 3.44.45; Phil Lovell =8th in the Vault with 4.80 and Ken Onuaguluchi 6th in the Discus with 51.88. Also Mike Alleyne - 400m ht of 49.32 and Stuart Paton - 1.50.01 800m ht.

* * *

At the Peugeot Talbot Games at C/Pal on 19/20 July Ernest Obeng confirmed his AAA form with Imoh and Asquith, winning the 100m in 10.39 while John Gladwin was the winner of the 1000m from a good domestic field in 2.19.69. It was also at this meeting that we saw the FIRST SUB 4 MINUTE MILE BY A BELGRAVIAN when Gary Staines chased home Steve Cram in a time of 3.59.24. Stu' Paton ran 4.07.89.

* * *

Edinburgh Games-23 July:100m E Obeng 4th 10.48

* * *

The Bislett Stadium again did the trick for JOHN GLADWIN, who reduced his 800m best to 1.46.11 in the process of winning the Oslo Games event on 27 July in a dip finish with Moussa Fall, following the disqualification of Mack. And John's verdict on the new surface is that it's not as fast as the old one!

* * *

At the Inter-Counties Championships held at Thurrock on 28 July, Ernie Obeng retained his 100m title from McFarlane in 10.9 into a strong wind as did Gary Staines in the Mile with 4.12.3. And, going one better than in the last two years, Ken Onuaguluchi made it a great Belgrave hat-trick by taking the Discus with a throw of 52.20.

* * *

At the IAC Grand Prix at C\Palace on 2 August, KEN ONUAGULUCHI just missed his own club record in the Discus with a fine throw of 55.18 in 4th place.

* * *

Budapest Grand Prix-4 Aug:Ernest Obeng maintained his good form with a 10.36 clocking behind Nagy and Lara, while John Gladwin ran what he admitted was probably his worst ever tactical race, placing 6th in 1.47.92. Then, over 2000m, there was an outstanding run not only from Steve Cram but also from Gary Staines who finished 6th in a remarkable 5.02.67, indicating that whatever Gary has achieved so far, he can surely reach greater heights over 3000m and 5000m eventually.

* * *

SCAAA v Comb. Serv. v Welsh AAA v Achilles - 7 Aug - C\Pal:3000mSc 3rd M Sinclair 8.56.9 - a great personal best from Mark who can make a big impact in this event. PV 1st P Lovell 4.90 - equals Phil's own club record. LJ\TJ T Taylor 1st\5th 7.43\14.37 SP\HT 4th\6th P McKay 13.91\46.54 DT 1st K Onuaguluchi 53.34.

Kodak Classic - Gateshead -9 August:100m 2nd E Obeng 10.58;DT 3rd K Onuaguluchi 54.62 U\20 Inter County Match-11 August: Jnrs: 100m'B' 1st P Goedluck 11.2; 200m 1st M Adams 22.1;3000m 4th A North 8.50.7. Yths: 100m'B' 2nd G Warr 12.0; 200m'B' 5th M Dooley 23.8; 1500m 3rd G Tiernan 4.11.8; 3000m 7th M Kinane 9.14.7; TJ 4th F Ogwuda 13.50.

* * *

At the AAA's U\20 Championships in Birmingham, John Regis placed a magnificent 2nd over 100m in 10.46w (10.45w ht) a run which clinched his selection for the European Junior Championships. Equally impressive was Marcus Adams with 3rd over 200m in 22.08 into a 4.56w!! And, backing these two up, we had Phil Goedluck running his fastest ever times - 10.79w for 7th in the 100 (10.75w ht) and 22.40 for 6th in the 200m (22.12 ht). Fast Juniors indeed! Our promising Triple Jumper, Festus Ogwuda placed 5th in the Youths event with 13.62, a fine performance.

* * *

At a Nottingham Open Combined Events meeting on 17/18 August, Festus Ogwuda completed a Decathlon with Junior implements in a club record score of 5010 points.

* * *

Home Junior International-Meadowbank-21 Aug: 100m 1st J Regis 10.87 200m 1st M Adams 21.54 (This was a further improvement in the Club Junior Record and an amazing run from a 17 year old into an unhelpful headwind!)

* * *

In one of the best kept secrets of the Europa Cup Final weekend, England 'B' were taking on France in Le Touquet. John Gladwin won selection for the 800m where he had a comfortable win in 1.46.88.*** Ernest Obeng ran a season's best of 10.28 in Zurich on 21 August, going on to 7th in the final in 10.43.

* * *

European Junior Championships, Cottbus, East Germany - 22/25 August - "Ein Kurzstreckenlauf Fantastisch": Well, what can one say about an individual Bronze Medal and Relay Gold by JOHN REGIS at these Championships. No doubt most of you were watching on TV and saw the way he ate up the ground in the last 30 metres of the 100m to come through to 3rd place behind Elliot Bunney and Havas of Hungary, in another Club Junior Record, after a cautious start because of a still tender hamstring. It was a fabulous run in any circumstances let alone those but perhaps John was even better on the final leg of the 4x100m where he brought the team home to the Gold Medal in a new UK Junior record of 39.80! I believe he must have come close to the club high jump record with that leap in front of the UK team in the stands at the end!!

* * *

At Crystal Palace on 26 August, JOHN GLADWIN completely dominated an International 800m for England against Poland, Hungary and Czechoslovakia, leading all the way to a time of 1.47.77. What with a victory lap in front of the home crowd and signing autographs afterwards, this was an afternoon for him to savour.

* * *

Tooting Bec Re-Opening Meeting - 24 August: KEN ONUAGULUCHI 56.08 - ANOTHER CLUB RECORD Leading Bels results:- 400m'B' 3. M Alleyne 49.2; 800m 5. S Paton 1.52.1 1500m 1. G Staines 3.42.7; 3000m 3. O Foote 8.17.4; 3000mSc 3. M Hayes 9.17.4; 110mH 7. M Lindsay-Bayley 15.6 SP 6. K Onuaguluchi 14.97pb, 9. M Small 13.01; DT 2. K Onuaguluchi 56.08, 10. A Featherstone 40.70, 11. M Small 40.32. Yths 100m 2. J Minetti 11.4; 1500m 1. G Tiernan 4.06.7, 2. M Kinane 4.09.9.

* * *

GLC Championships - 31 August - Bels Medallists:- Jnrs: 800m 3. R O'Hara 2.01.1; LJ/TJ 1/2 F Ogwuda 6.19/13.33. Yths 100m/200m 2/2 J Minetti 11.9/23.4; 800m 3. M Kinane 2.01.8

* * *

Whistlestop 100's from Ernie: Koblenz-28 Aug-6th 10.52: Riccione-31 Aug-3rd 10.53: Rieti-4 Sept-3rd 10.39.*** On 15 Sept at C\Palace Phil Goedluck won over 100m at the U\20 Inter Area match in 11.03.

* * *

John Gladwin and Gary Staines brought their most successful seasons to a close on a short tour of China with the England U/23 team. In Beijing on 8 September, John won the 800m in 1.47.35 with the European Champion in his wake, while Gary won over 1500m in a tactical 3.51.15. Then, 3 days later in Nanjing, John completed a fine double in his second fastest time - 1.46.42 with Ferner again behind, while Mark Rowland, this time reversed placings with Gary in the 1500m, Gary clocking 3.54.10 in another slow race.

OLIVER SOUTHERN 10 KM SILVER

It was inevitable that when Oliver Foote found himself in a higher class 10k field than the County Championships he would come up with something a little closer to his potential. And so it was at the Southern Counties Championship at Crystal Palace on 11 September where he ran to a fine Silver Medal and a time of 29.49.4 - fastest by a Belgravian for 17 years! It was also at this meeting that Matthew Kinane confirmed his progress this year by breaking the longstanding Club Youths Mile Record with 4.26.0 in 7th place in the Peter Coe mile.

* * *

International Youth Friendship Games - Crystal Palace - 29 September - JOHN REGIS 20.78!!! John Regis was really in a purple patch at the end of the season and his final race produced a brilliant 20.78 clocking over 200m to succeed Ernie Obeng as Club record holder; I believe that only 3 British Juniors have ever gone faster. And in case you didn't know it, John is still a Junior next season!! Another spectacular performance came in the 4x200m when Mike Bazire, Phil Goedluck and Marcus Adams joined John to clock a time that only 2 UK National teams and a Haringey club side, amongst British teams, have ever beaten. There were some other pretty good performances as well - if that's not an understatement - with Marcus Adams, Phil Goedluck and Youth Joe Minetti all sprinting magnificently. Results: (Non-Scorers in brackets):- 100m 1. How the Club Mile Record Has Progressed

Club records were not formalised until after 1950 and our early history is a little short on details such as times. However, it is reported that A H N Edward, Hon. Sec of the club from 1887-1898, was "a sound 4.30 miler" and A D Pyer is believed to have run 4.22 in the early 1920's.(Confirmation of any times from this period would be welcomed).But the following is probably accurate:-

Mark Sinclair has run p.b's in the 1500m, 3000m, 5000m, and 3000s/c this summer. His 1500m time of 3.48.5 is 0.1 faster than John Bicourts p.b. and as John has been advising Mark on hurdling technique it is pleasing to see Mark move to 3rd spot on our all time 3000s/c rankings with a fine 8.56.9. Our strength in this event is considerable with Mike Hayes close to a sub 9.00 effort.

Report · page 63

WITH THE LADIES

Winter reports by Harry Porter.

It has been quite an exciting winter. This year was the first time we had had representatives (Rosemary Honeychurch, Vicky Mitchell and Jenny Webb) in the Southern Inter Counties and National Inter Counties Cross Country Championships. Also the first time we have gained team medals in County Cross Country Championships (the Surrey Intermediate Team of Jenny Webb, Gail Porter and Andrea Coore who finished 3rd team). The first time we had had an individual winner in the Surrey League Cross Country Championships (Rosemary Honeychurch) and also we had team winners in the same race (Rosemary Honeychurch, Vicky Mitchell, Jenny Webb and Debbie Hankins). We also finished a Senior Team in the National Cross Country Championships (37th). In the Surrey League Rosemary Honeychurch finished 3rd individual over the 4 races and in the Inters Jenny Webb was 5th and Vicky Mitchell was 6th.

Our Walkers have been doing well with Gail Porter and Rachael Parrott (who won a race in the Cambridge Harriers Walking League) well to the fore.

Our winter training has been quite well attended with good attendances on Saturday afternoons and Sunday mornings at Belgrave Hall, and Sunday mornings at Battersea Park and Wednesday Evening Circuit Training Sessions have also been well attended.

Winter reports by Harry Porter.

Rosemary HoneychurchVicky MitchellJenny WebbGail PorterAndrea CooreDebbie HankinsRachael ParrottHarry Porter

Feature · page 65

'DYNAMITE' ROSE

ROSEMARY HONEYCHURCH: Alias "Dynamite Rose" is "Belles" leading middle distance star, and is a 21 year old student at Whitelands College where she is studying for a degree in sociology. How many of you have taken 32 secs. and 65 secs. off your P.B.s for 1500m and 3000m in one season? "Dynamite" did just that!!

At the start of the 1984 season her best 1500m was 5.17 - at the end - 4.45. Her best 3000m was 11.07 - at the end - 10.02. Her coach (since last April), Derek Crookes says she is a "Dedicated trainer" who does everything laid down in training schedules, and is an out and out fighter. 1984 saw her win the Surrey County Senior 3000m and this was followed up by representing Surrey in the same event. Her winter "hobby" is trying to run her coach into the deck over a hard 3 to 4 mile run most Saturdays at Wimbledon. She represented Surrey in the Inter-Counties C.C.Champs 1985 but her best run was to chase Ann Ford in the C.P. 6km road race at the end of December.

Rosemary HoneychurchDerek CrookesAnn Ford
See it on the printed page →
Report · page 67

SUMMER ROUNDUP

DEREK JONES

After the good winter season with the Seniors coming 2nd in the Surrey Cross Country League and finishing a team in the National, the start of the Track season was a bit disappointing. In our two warm up meetings we finished 5th out of 6 and 6th out of 7 and we were a disappointing 2nd in our first Southern League to put us in 9th spot in the table. Even after the 2nd Southern League we were 7th and in the next 3 matches we had to meet all the teams above us. However, everybody got stuck in and competed out of their skins in the last four matches with the result that we did what we wanted to do and got promotion to Division 2. So well done to all concerned. It really was a fantastic result, especially those last 4 matches in the Southern League.

I would like to mention the determination of Wendy Dunsford who, over the 6 years has competed in a remarkable 44 Southern League meetings out of a total of 46. The September meeting was her 39th consecutive Southern League. In the Thameside League the girls finished 5th, but the Juniors were level on League points with Camberley and just lost out on insufficient match points (by about 7½). Just one mention which I almost forgot and that is the Junior Relay squad who in the last 2 seasons crossed the line in first place in League Races (Southern & Thameside) and Inter Club matches on no less than 22 occasions. (Unfortunately one resulted in a disqualification). They also gained a Bronze in the Southern Counties Womens AAA as well as coming 5th in the WAAA Championships.

Finally, I would like to thank everybody who came along to give their help and support. Please keep it coming. I would also like to thank everybody who has contributed over the six years I have been Ladies Secretary. I think I can safely say that I leave the section in a healthy state, which is no thanks to me, but to the efforts of all the athletes and those who have helped.

I would like to thank Frank Simmons and John Gould for their help and coaching skills. To my successor Harry Porter I wish Good Luck and thank you for the help you have given me over the past few seasons. I hope you get the same co-operation as I do. I will certainly be on hand with help and advice.

Good Luck for 1986.

DEREK JONES

Wendy DunsfordFrank SimmonsJohn GouldHarry PorterDerek Jones

PROMOTION ENSURED

7th SEPTEMBER 1985 BATTERSEA PARK SOUTHERN LEAGUE DIV.3 (SOUTH) MATCH 6 1.BELGRAVE 208; 2.Hastings 180½; 3.Surrey Beagles 142; 4.Havant 116½; 5.Dartford 73.

This result kept us in 2nd place and clinched promotion. With 4 Club Records, 1 Club Record equalled, and about 12 Personal Bests you can't ask for more than that at the end of a long season. A good performance from Diane Bowen breaking the Club Record in the Junior Ladies Discus by nearly 4 metres, this could possibly put her among the top 20 Juniors. The other record breakers were Vicky Mitchell in the 800m (Intermediate), Joanne Hilliard in the High Jump and the 4x400m Relay Team of Rosemary Honeychurch, Gabrielle Collinson, Dezrine Conard and Vicky Mitchell. (I am afraid I have mislaid the split times of this, but I do know that Vicky's final leg was 61.8). Gail equalled her own 400m Hurdles record and the most notable personal best was Wendy Dunsford's 11.66 in the Shot pushing her team mate into the B string. Incidentally this was Wendy's 39th consecutive Southern League Meeting being present since June 1979 and since we started has only missed two Southern Leagues. Not a bad record and Wendy will always try whatever the circumstances.

Feature · page 70

JOANNE HILLIARD

Born in New Zealand on 1st March 1962 Joanne Hilliard moved to the U.K. at the age of 7. She became interested in athletics and started competing at club level at the age of 16, starting as an all-rounder before progressing on to training specifically for the high jump

She trained with Ron Murrey for 5 years and gained her first International vest in July 1983 when she represented Great Britain in the G.B v France match. Further International appearances followed including a match in Norway in August 1983. It is perhaps surprising that with this background and a p.b. of 1.83 that the following April she should decide to give up the high jump. In September 1984 she decided to take up the discus and joined Mike Winches squad. She was among the first of Mike's group to start training in the Belgrave Hall gymnasium and set the travelling record by commuting three nights per week from St. Albans to Wimbledon for strength training. It was a natural progression when she moved to London early in 1985 that she should join Belgrave. Soon after she became engaged to another of Mike's squad- Blackheath Harrier and 80m+ javelin thrower Daryl Brand. Joanne has shown great promise in the discus and it is on the cards that both she and Daryl will see International competition in 1986.

Biographical details, Height - 6'2", Weight - Mind your own business! P.B's. High Jump - 1.83(1983). 1.80(1985). Club Record. Discus - 47.80(1985). Club Record Shot - 12.36(1985). Club Record Javelin - 29.70(1985).

Joanne HilliardRon MurreyMike WinchesDaryl Brand

27th/28th APRIL 1985 CROYDON ARENA SURREY COUNTY CHAMPIONSHIPS Inter Ladies 3000m: 2.J.Webb 11:07.00; Senior Ladies 3000m: 2.R.Honeychurch 10:40.0; Junior Ladies 800m: 4. (heat) J.Stone 2:36.9; Junior Ladies 2500m Walk: 2.R.Parrott 14:40.2 (3rd in open); Inter-Ladies 3000m Walk: 2.G.Porter 17.12.2 (3rd in open);

In these Championships we had 7 people entered, 5 competed and 4 gained medals (6 medals in all).

A good run by Jenny but not so good by Rosemary suffering from a viral complaint.

17th JULY 1985 BATTERSEA PARK MIDWEEK LEAGUE 1.BELGRAVE 83; 2.Herc.Wimb. 74; 3.Kingston 55; 4.Epsom & Ewell 39; 5.Walton 21.

Good running especially in both relays with the Seniors managing to stay ahead in time. Also good middle distance running from Vicky Mitchell and Gabrielle Collinson (her first attempt at 1500m).

Column · page 76

VETS. CORNER

OVER 50'S WIN

11th NOVEMBER 1984 RICHMOND PARK SURREY VETS C.C. CHAMPS.

Excellent runs by Pat O'Connor, Keith Sinclair and Tony Verdie in a star studded field, but it was the good packing by our over 50 men which provided the team gold over a hard undulating 10,000m course.

M.40-49: 1.S.Warzee (Guild. & G) 32.04; 2.R.Gevers (SLH) 32.35; 3.F.Bell (Woking) 33.23; 4.D.Claxton (HHH) 33.51; Bels: 12.P.O'Connor 35.05; 17.A.Verdie 35.56; 18.K.Sinclair 35.56; 31.J.Wasbrough 37.29; 38.W.Laws 38.35; 40.F.Barrett 38.44; 44.M.Hanson 39.18; 57.A.Stone 40.52; 76.E.Thorpe 42.56.

Teams: 1.Woking 19; 2.HHH 22; 3.Ranelagh 38; 4.BELGRAVE 47; 5.Herc.Wim. 56; 6.SLH 59.

M.over 50: 1.M.Firth (SLH) 35.53; 2.J.Chandler (Wav) 36.49; 3.J.Heywood (HHH) 37.22; 4.J.Phelan (HW) 37.35; ... Bels: 9.C.Walker 39.16; 10.B.Stroud 39.58; 11.C.Henn 40.07; 27.G.Pearson 46.39; 32.C.Manning 49.35.

Teams: 1.BELGRAVE 30; 2.SLH 33; 3.Ranelagh 42; 4.Mitcham 53.

11th MAY 1985 WIMBLEDON VETS A.C. 10 MILE Over 75 runners competed the Vets A.C. 10 miles Road Race Championship held over an accurately measured course around the road side and foot paths of Wimbledon Common. 53 year old Laurie O'Hara stuck to the two younger Hercules runners Kortenray and Fuller as the race hotted up over the final mile. Kortenray's finishing power gave him the verdict by seconds when he beat Fuller and O'Hara in a sprint to the line. However, Laurie took the Championship, as the first two were not qualified for the honour.

Results: 1.M.Kortenray (HW) 54.46; 2.M.Fuller (HW) 54.48; 3.L.O'Hara (Belgrave) 54.50. Bels: 7.L.Mann 59.31; 9.J.Wasbrough 60.03; 12.T.Stone 60.15.

13th SEPTEMBER 1985 CRAWLEY S.C.V. AAA'S 10 MILE CHAMPIONSHIP Results: 1.L.Roberts (Blackheath) 50.43; 2.B.Buonvino (Dart) 51.52; 3.W.Davies (Hillingdon) 53.55. Bels: 13.P.O'Connor 56.15; 26.J.Wasbrough 58.13; 47.F.Barrett 60.43; 61.K.Duckett 62.43; 71.E.Stroud 63.27 p.b. 91.C.Cross 65.37;

Teams: 1.Blackheath 14; 2.Hillingdon 20; 3.Cambridge 52; 6.BELGRAVE 86.

THE AMAZING LAURIE O'HARA

At the age of 53 do we detect that Laurie O'Hara is finally slowing down? Well only a bit. He's still managed to pull off track championships and on the road has troubled men 30 years his junior. For example:-

Hillingdon ½ marathon - Open - 6th 1hr 13.02 Belgrave 1500m Champs - 4:14.0 Battersea 5¼ Mile 16.7.85. 1st Vet 27.36 Southern Vets Champs. Crawley 27.7.85. 1500 1st (o/50) 4.20 WL Vets Championship 31.7.85. 10,000m 1st o/50 33.20 West London Stadium - Open 3000m 7.8.85. 9:06.0 Alperton. 4¼ Mile Open Road Race 20.8.85. 12th. 22.31 Tooting Bec. Southern Vets Champs. 18.9.85. 1st (o/50) 33.19.

And thats only a sample!

AND SNOWY BROOKS

You will read elsewhere of the equally excellent performances of that one man Vets team Snowy Brooks - European medals and all. He has decimated our Vets records this summer and seven of them now stand in his name. (Brian Pritchard says Snowy won't beat him over 1500!)

100m 12.0, 110mH 17.1, (16.7 awaiting confirmation), P.V. 3.20, L.J. 6.42, J.T. 46.78, Pentathalon 3478 pts, Decathalon 4993 pts.

Records · page 77

ALL TIME TOP 10.

Steve Williams

ALL TIME TOP 10. Updated by Steve Williams

The injection of new blood into the track and field team over the last four seasons, the unearthing of one or two more top performances from the past and improvements from already established members have meant a substantial number of changes to the Top Ten published in the April '82 magazine.

Evidence of current track and field standards is provided by 72 new marks from the past four years, including 31 in the season just gone - not bad from a total listing of 212 performances. Those with eagle eyes wishing to compare this list with the previous one will note some imperial distance conversion amendments - these are now more accurate. As before, known wind assisted marks in the sprints and lateral jumps are excluded, as are indoor marks, and, to the best of my knowledge, only 1st claim members are listed.

Continued on page 77.

Photograph · page 79

Above. Andy North seen here representing Surrey in the Junior Inter Counties broke through last winter to finish a highly respectable 20th in the Junior National C.C.Champs and put his excellent winters background to good use by breaking the Club's long standing Junior 5000m record with a fine 14.55.

Andy NorthBill Laws
See it on the printed page →
Photograph · page 79

Back Cover. The South of Thames Junior C.C.Championship provided a unique and exciting spectacle for Belgrave supporters when at the 2 mile mark our men held the first 4 places in the 300 strong field. Our picture shows eventual winner Oliver Foote in the lead from Mark Sinclair(6th), John Gladwin(2nd), and Dave North(7th), the team's winning score being the best ever recorded in the 50 year history of this race - a fitting result with Gordon Biscoe as the S.O.T. President.

Oliver FooteMark SinclairJohn GladwinDave NorthGordon BiscoeBill Laws

The Belgravian was Belgrave Harriers’ club gazette (1925–2009). Scans by Alan Mead, club historian (belgraveharriers.info). Text transcribed exactly as printed; from 1976 onwards private addresses and phone numbers are withheld. Spotted a mistake, or want an entry removed? Email belgravian@belgraveharriers.com.
